FILE C:\Users\Jorge\.agents\skills\audit-context-building\SKILL.md
---
name: audit-context-building
description: Understand a codebase before looking for bugs in it - what each function assumes, what it guarantees, and what it depends on elsewhere. Use when starting an audit, threat model, or architecture review on unfamiliar code, and before any vulnerability-hunting pass.
allowed-tools: Task Read Grep Glob
---

# Audit Context Building

Build understanding, not verdicts. This runs before anyone hunts for bugs, and feeds that work.

## When to Use

At the start of an audit, a threat model, or an architecture review, when the code is unfamiliar. Also when
an earlier pass produced findings nobody could judge, because no one had mapped out how the system fits
together.

## When NOT to Use

Do not name vulnerabilities, suggest fixes, write proofs-of-concept, or rate severity. Those belong to the
hunting phase, which runs next and with the whole picture in hand. When the code counts on something and
nothing checks it, record that plainly and move on - whether it matters is decided later.

Not worth the tokens on code you already understand.

## Do not analyze in this context

The analysis is long, and this context needs to survive to use it. Dispatch it:

- **A codebase, or more than one function** - run `/audit-context-building:audit-context <path>`. It orients,
  analyzes each function in its own subagent, and writes `audit-context/DOSSIER.md` plus one file per
  function under `audit-context/functions/`. Only compact records return here.
- **A single function** - dispatch the `audit-context-building:function-analyzer` agent at it. It writes its
  prose to disk and returns a record.

Then work from what comes back: the index, the unenforced assumptions, the open questions. Read a function's
file when you need its detail.

The workflow is what enforces this, not this text: a subagent bound to a return schema cannot return prose.
Treat this section as routing, and route.

## What comes back, and how to read it

Each record lists what must always be true (with the line that shows it), what the function takes on faith
(with whatever establishes it), which functions it calls and what it needs from each, and anything still
unclear. The dossier adds the rules that span several functions, who can reach what, and where the
complicated parts cluster.

Two things matter more than the rest:

- **Assumptions marked `nothing found`.** The code counts on something being true and nothing anywhere makes
  it true. This is the most useful thing to hand the hunting phase.
- **The open questions.** An honest list of what is still unclear beats a confident answer that turns out to
  be wrong. Carry them forward instead of closing them out.

Where two records disagree, both are quoted rather than quietly reconciled. That is a fact about the code,
not a flaw in the analysis.

## The format

[ANALYSIS_FORMAT.md](resources/ANALYSIS_FORMAT.md) defines it, and
[FUNCTION_MICRO_ANALYSIS_EXAMPLE.md](resources/FUNCTION_MICRO_ANALYSIS_EXAMPLE.md) works through examples in
C and Solidity. Read them when extending this plugin or deciding whether a record can be trusted.

The format is the same whatever the target. What changes is what fills each slot, and what counts as a call
you cannot see inside. [DOMAIN_NOTES.md](resources/DOMAIN_NOTES.md) maps that across smart contracts, C and
C++, decompiled firmware, and web services - read it when the target is not plain source code.

**The rule that matters most: follow the calls.** Whether a function is correct usually depends on something
another function does, and you cannot see that from the caller alone. A limit looks enforced because the
value came back from a function whose name suggests it was checked. So read the function being called, follow
every path through it rather than only the one that succeeds, and say what makes each assumption true. When
nothing does, use those words: `nothing found`. Every claim cites a line, or becomes an open question.
FILE omerta-contracts\SECURITY-REVIEW-POLICY.md
# OMERTA security review policy

**Owner decision - 2026-09-08.** Security reviews are performed by agents using the evidence requirements
below. This is the current policy for new work, release reviews, and material changes to previously
reviewed code. It supersedes older runbook statements about who must perform a security review.

The owner has authorized this review workflow. Agents proceed with code inspection, isolated proofs,
tests, fuzzing, invariant checks, static analysis, remediation, and verification within the requested
scope. A completed review is evidence for its exact scope and revision; it is not a blanket audit of
OMERTA, an assertion that no defects remain, or authorization to activate another rail.

## Pinned methods

Use the relevant methods from all three repositories. The 2026-09-08 method baseline was verified with
`git rev-parse HEAD` in the local checkouts under `output/audit-methods/`:

| Source | Pinned commit | Application |
| --- | --- | --- |
| [pashov/skills](https://github.com/pashov/skills/tree/c577eb7799c349de0acb187ba00ca98e14e436fd) | `c577eb7799c349de0acb187ba00ca98e14e436fd` | Adversarial passes over access control, execution traces, invariants, economic assumptions, boundaries, and periphery; a concrete trace or proof distinguishes a finding from a lead. |
| [PlamenTSV/plamen](https://github.com/PlamenTSV/plamen/tree/795962b96e254f2e423a2635fe7f8cb8ea1e6d69) | `795962b96e254f2e423a2635fe7f8cb8ea1e6d69` | Protocol-specific breadth and depth, state/accounting transitions, callback and transfer behavior, and verified exploit hypotheses. |
| [trailofbits/skills](https://github.com/trailofbits/skills/tree/d3323cefbcf645678b8dc481de204b02ad3d02dc) | `d3323cefbcf645678b8dc481de204b02ad3d02dc` | Build context and trust-boundary maps first; apply specification comparison, property/invariant testing, static-analysis triage, and false-positive checks where relevant. |

These are method sources, not evidence that a tool or a whole upstream orchestration ran. Record the
exact skills/passes actually used. Keep adaptations explicit: available agent slots, native toolchain,
language, dependency setup, and applicable scope may differ from upstream examples. Do not silently
replace a pin with a moving branch; record a new pin and the reason when adopting an update.

## Review package

Every release review retains a cohesive package containing:

1. **Source and scope.** Full source commit, dirty/clean state, hashes for reviewed working-tree files
   when applicable, file/function inventory, included dependencies, excluded surfaces, release phase,
   and the behavior/properties being claimed. Pin contract compiler, optimizer/EVM settings, dependency
   versions, artifact/runtime hashes, chain, addresses and deployment parameters when applicable.
2. **System model.** Entry points and callers, assets/liabilities, storage and event authority, replay
   domains, privilege boundaries, external calls, off-chain signer/indexer assumptions, and failure
   behavior. Follow callees; do not treat a helper's name as proof that it validates its input.
3. **Adversarial review.** Separate passes over the material trust boundaries, followed by cross-checking
   their findings. Include malicious recipients/adapters, reentrancy, replay, rounding, configuration
   drift, stale reads, cross-system state changes and recovery where they apply. Mark checklist items
   that do not apply and explain why; do not manufacture coverage for unrelated mechanisms.
4. **Executed evidence.** Commands, tool versions, run date, input/seed/run budget, exit status, and
   retained outputs for meaningful unit/integration regressions, executable exploit proofs, stateful
   fuzzing and invariants. Exercise persistence/concurrency against PostgreSQL where correctness
   depends on its locks or transactions; an in-memory test does not prove those properties. Use real
   supported contract compilers and include all in-scope suites. Record a missing or failed run plainly.
5. **Static-analysis triage.** Retain the raw diagnostics and disposition by finding/rule. Verify each
   actionable result against reachable code and invariants; document false positives, exclusions and
   tool failures. A successful tool invocation is not equivalent to a clean analysis.
6. **Findings and remediation.** Stable IDs, severity and rationale, prerequisites, affected source
   references, reproducible trace/proof, actual impact, correction, and retest evidence. Keep leads
   distinct from confirmed defects. Preserve resolved findings and invalidated hypotheses with their
   reasoning; do not erase the record when code is fixed.
7. **Scope-specific conclusion.** State what is ready, what remains open or unverified, accepted residual
   risks, and precisely which revision/phase the conclusion covers. Link the evidence package from the
   release manifest and retain its content hash.

For code where a category is inapplicable, retain an explicit rationale instead of an empty claim of
coverage. For example, a documentation-only change does not require Solidity fuzzing; a fee-to-NFT
release does require its payment, authorization, callback, indexing and recovery paths to be exercised.

## Completion and change control

- An in-scope critical or high finding must be fixed and retested before the affected release is ready.
  Record the disposition and rationale for every other finding, including any accepted residual risk.
  A lead or a failed/unavailable test remains visible and limits the conclusion accordingly.
- Conclusions apply to the exact reviewed source and configuration. Material changes to contracts,
  signers, authorization, accounting, replay domains, adapters/oracles, persistence or write routes
  reopen the affected review. Reassess shared dependencies and rerun the checks impacted by the change.
- Historic reports and test totals remain point-in-time evidence. Preserve them with their dates and
  hashes; do not present them as proof for changed source or rewrite them to imply a newer review.
- A report hash field is a reference, not a verifier. Check that it resolves to the retained package
  and that the package's scope/source match the release. Existing deployment wrapper field names such
  as `CORE_AUDIT_REPORT_SHA256` and `CORE_SIGNER_AUDIT_INCLUDED` retain their operational meaning under
  this policy: the hash identifies the scoped review package and the signer must actually be included.

## Deployment remains a separate operation

Review completion does not broadcast, fund, upgrade, configure, or enable a contract. Keep the
deployment runbook's exact source/bytecode verification, Safe owner/threshold and signer separation,
transaction simulation, caps, recipients, metadata routing, chain/database isolation, confirmation
policy, monitoring and recovery requirements. Owner launch acceptance and any applicable platform
routing approval remain separate from security evidence. A character NFT review does not clear token
issuance, liquidity, bonds, RWA delivery, THE BANK, or withdrawals outside its declared scope.

Canonical entry points: [contract deployment](DEPLOYMENT.md), [chain activation](../CHAIN-DEPLOY.md),
[launch readiness](../LAUNCH-READINESS.md), and [character NFT activation](CHARACTER-NFT-LAUNCH.md).

omerta-contracts\audits\2026-10-01-red-team\market-review.md:115:Daybreak identified a separate generic-dependency issue after the initial guarded-route pass: `ContinuousClearingAuction.lbpInitializationParams` subtracted the fee controller's reported amount without bounding it, while `sweepCurrency` already clamps the same amount to currency raised. Smuggling has geography and infrastructure.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · The Port</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/02-economy/02-economy-11-estate-auctions.png" alt="WEALTH NEEDS A HOME."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>WEALTH NEEDS A HOME.</h3><p>The Estate is a wealth sink, a display surface and a market endpoint. The Auction House supports bidding and player consignment, so rare holdings can change hands without an admin inventing a price.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Estate / Auction House</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/02-economy/02-economy-12-portfolio.png" alt="THE BOOK OUTLIVES THE SCORE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE / GATED RAILS</span><h3>THE BOOK OUTLIVES THE SCORE.</h3><p>OMERTÀ’s long capital game connects the Portfolio, Dynasty Fund, Family books, Street Deeds and the Megaproject. Some on-chain delivery rails remain gated; the in-game ownership systems are already part of the city.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · capital systems</small></div></article></div></section><section><header><p>BLOOD / LAW</p><h2>BLOOD &amp; THE LAW</h2><div>Violence, contracts, protection, consequences, investigation, and prison.</div></header><div class="grid"><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-01-wet-work.png" alt="VIOLENCE HAS INTENT."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>VIOLENCE HAS INTENT.</h3><p>In OMERTÀ, “attack” is not one generic button. Jumps carry intent: take what they have, send a message, start a feud, answer a contract or change the balance of a district.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 · jumps</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-02-contract-board.png" alt="A BOUNTY IS A POT."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>A BOUNTY IS A POT.</h3><p>A hit contract is not flavor text. It is a funded pot with a target, terms, expiry, settlement and refund path. The board makes the city’s grudges liquid.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 · Contract Board</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-03-hitmen.png" alt="THE CITY TRACKS WHO FINISHES JOBS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE CITY TRACKS WHO FINISHES JOBS.</h3><p>Players can become the weapon. OMERTÀ combines contract fulfillment, NPC hitmen and an account-level assassin reputation ladder where finished work follows the bloodline.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 · hitmen</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-04-protection.png" alt="THE TARGET CAN HIRE BACK."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE TARGET CAN HIRE BACK.</h3><p>The hunted are not passive content. Bodyguard and hired-gun markets let another player sell protection, change an attacker’s expected value and make survival somebody’s paid job.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 · bodyguards</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-05-vendettas.png" alt="A GRUDGE CAN ESCALATE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>A GRUDGE CAN ESCALATE.</h3><p>A fight can end—or harden into a vendetta. Escalation tiers, the sit-down and the blood-debt board turn repeated violence into diplomacy, leverage and inherited history.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 · vendettas</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-06-safehouse.png" alt="A SHIELD. NOT A BUNKER."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>A SHIELD. NOT A BUNKER.</h3><p>The safehouse is deliberately a shield, not a bunker. It can stop hostile action, but it also blocks routes that turn holdings into cash. Safety has opportunity cost.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 / §3.7</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-07-death-estate.png" alt="WHEN THE BODY GOES, THE BOOKS OPEN."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>WHEN THE BODY GOES, THE BOOKS OPEN.</h3><p>Permadeath works only if the accounting survives it. OMERTÀ resolves death on the server, settles the estate and carries forward the liabilities and relationships the heir is meant to inherit.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 · death and estate</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-08-rico.png" alt="THE CASE BUILDS WHILE YOU PLAY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE CASE BUILDS WHILE YOU PLAY.</h3><p>The law is a progression system. Heat feeds an investigation meter; bribes and retainers buy room; indictment can reach the courtroom, forfeiture and prison. The Bureau remembers patterns.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Law / RICO</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-09-courtroom.png" alt="PLEAD. PICK A JURY. GO TO TRIAL."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>PLEAD. PICK A JURY. GO TO TRIAL.</h3><p>An indictment is not a single jail roll. Plea, jury and trial routes interact with retainers, informants, witness protection and the evidence the Bureau assembled.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · courtroom</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-10-pen.png" alt="PRISON IS ANOTHER GAME."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>PRISON IS ANOTHER GAME.</h3><p>Jail is a status. The Pen is a system: yard politics, work, commissary, protection, bribes, punishment, factions, the burner phone and solo or co-op breakout attempts.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · The Pen</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-11-dueling.png" alt="SKILL GETS A RATING."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>SKILL GETS A RATING.</h3><p>The Dueling Ladder turns controlled violence into a ranked discipline: ELO, divisions, weapon styles and a seasonal belt make the best fighter answerable to the next challenger.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Dueling Ladder</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-12-risk-to-earn.png" alt="VALUE IS EXPOSED BECAUSE IT CAN MOVE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>VALUE IS EXPOSED BECAUSE IT CAN MOVE.</h3><p>OMERTÀ’s economy is designed around exposure: loot the living, launder at a real location, move money through transit, and choose when protection is worth losing access to your own holdings.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.7 · Risk-to-Earn</small></div></article></div></section><section><header><p>POWER</p><h2>ORGANIZATIONS &amp; POWER</h2><div>Crews, Families, territory, government, recruiting, secrets, and loyalty.</div></header><div class="grid"><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/04-organizations/04-organizations-01-crews.png" alt="FOUR PLAYERS CAN BECOME A MACHINE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>FOUR PLAYERS CAN BECOME A MACHINE.</h3><p>A crew is the first real organization: recruit by name, assign trust, tackle shared objectives and build the working unit a future Family can depend on.</p><small>AGENTS.md · organization playbook</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/04-organizations/04-organizations-02-crew-heists.png" alt="TWELVE JOBS. EVERYBODY HAS A ROLE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>TWELVE JOBS. EVERYBODY HAS A ROLE.</h3><p>Crew heists are not a party-size checkbox. Twelve jobs ask for roles, casing and execution; the inside job, the rat and the fence can turn the same score into a different story.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Crew Heists</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/04-organizations/04-organizations-03-family.png" alt="A CREW WORKS. A FAMILY RULES."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>A CREW WORKS. A FAMILY RULES.</h3><p>A Family adds a treasury, command ranks, contracts, turf income, war bodies and Commission politics. It is not a guild chat. It is a government with books.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 / §3.8</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/04-organizations/04-organizations-04-territory.png" alt="THE MAP PAYS WHO HOLDS IT."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE MAP PAYS WHO HOLDS IT.</h3><p>Territory makes the map economic. Control changes perks and income, but fortification, upkeep, Bureau pressure and rival raids make every claim a position to defend.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Territory</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/04-organizations/04-organizations-05-wars.png" alt="POLITICS ENDS. THE LEDGER CONTINUES."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>POLITICS ENDS. THE LEDGER CONTINUES.</h3><p>Family wars are lifecycle-managed conflicts with stakes beyond a kill counter. Turf, treasury, bodies, treaties and future retaliation all survive the headline battle.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 / §3.8</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/04-organizations/04-organizations-06-commission.png" alt="THE STRONGEST FAMILIES WRITE THE WEEK."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE STRONGEST FAMILIES WRITE THE WEEK.</h3><p>The Commission turns seasonal Family standing into temporary political power. Seats can propose decrees, risk deposits, invoke vetoes, levy the city and face an override.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Commission</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/04-organizations/04-organizations-07-diplomacy.png" alt="NOT EVERY BORDER NEEDS A BULLET."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>NOT EVERY BORDER NEEDS A BULLET.</h3><p>Diplomacy gives Families tools between silence and war: pacts, coalitions and negotiated alignment make trust a strategic asset—and betrayal a recorded one.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Diplomacy</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/04-organizations/04-organizations-08-sovereignty.png" alt="HOLD THE STRONGHOLD. SURVIVE THE SIEGE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>HOLD THE STRONGHOLD. SURVIVE THE SIEGE.</h3><p>Sovereignty raises the stakes from corners to strongholds. Sieges, income, NPC occupation, frontier pressure and The Uprising turn the city itself into a contested actor.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Sovereignty / Uprising</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/04-organizations/04-organizations-09-recruiting.png" alt="RECRUIT PEOPLE WHO STAY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE / CONDITIONAL</span><h3>RECRUIT PEOPLE WHO STAY.</h3><p>OMERTÀ rewards qualified organization-building, not clicks. Retained, minted, level-8+ human recruits count toward the Capo License. Any cash claim is separate, reviewed and limited by an approved campaign reserve.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Capo’s License</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/04-organizations/04-organizations-10-phone-broadcast.png" alt="THE CITY CAN CALL BACK."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE CITY CAN CALL BACK.</h3><p>The cellphone makes social access earned: establish contact before a DM, respect permanent blocks, and turn real player history into Broadcast cards, profiles and rivalry posters that carry attribution.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.4 / §3.8</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/04-organizations/04-organizations-11-wire-secrets.png" alt="INFORMATION HAS A MARKET."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>INFORMATION HAS A MARKET.</h3><p>OMERTÀ has an information economy: wiretaps, sweeps, trace risk, dossiers, disinformation, informants, blackmail and standing watches. Sometimes the strongest move is knowing first.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Wire / Secrets</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/04-organizations/04-organizations-12-marriage-soldiers.png" alt="LOYALTY GETS A NAME."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>LOYALTY GETS A NAME.</h3><p>Families are made of people, not slots. Marriage, the consigliere and named soldiers create durable household and command relationships—with permadeath when the city takes one of them.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · social depth</small></div></article></div></section><section><header><p>VICE / CITY</p><h2>VICE &amp; THE LIVING CITY</h2><div>Casino tables, competition careers, nightlife, NPC power, and a changing city.</div></header><div class="grid"><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-01-casino.png" alt="THE HOUSE HAS MANY ROOMS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE HOUSE HAS MANY ROOMS.</h3><p>The Casino is a network, not one slot machine: craps, Numbers, blackjack, back-room dice, poker, tournaments, weekly fights, THE TRACK and THE FUTURITY.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Casino</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-02-tables.png" alt="THE ODDS ARE PART OF THE WORLD."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE ODDS ARE PART OF THE WORLD.</h3><p>Craps, Numbers, blackjack and back-room dice do not collapse into the same animation. Each table has its own risk, timing, bankroll and counterparty shape.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · casino tables</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-03-poker.png" alt="THE CARDS REMEMBER THE TABLE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE CARDS REMEMBER THE TABLE.</h3><p>Poker in OMERTÀ runs from heads-up pressure to ring tables and a full tournament bracket. The cards are server-dealt; the reputation belongs to the player.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · poker</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-04-track.png" alt="BET THE HORSE. WATCH THE CIRCUIT."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>BET THE HORSE. WATCH THE CIRCUIT.</h3><p>THE TRACK is the betting surface for a persistent horse economy. Form, training, breeding, circuit results and THE FUTURITY give the odds a world behind them.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Track / Futurity</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-05-stable.png" alt="BUY. TRAIN. BREED. RACE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>BUY. TRAIN. BREED. RACE.</h3><p>The Stable is a career: acquire a horse, train it, breed a line, enter the circuit, negotiate match races and chase THE STAKES. The asset has a history before the wager.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Stable</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-06-street-races.png" alt="TUNE THE CAR. RISK THE TITLE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>TUNE THE CAR. RISK THE TITLE.</h3><p>Street racing connects garage ownership to a competitive ladder: tune the car, choose the circuit or a player wager, burn nitrous, risk a pink slip and chase THE GRAND PRIX.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Street Races</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-07-boxing.png" alt="BUILD A BOXER. DEFEND THE BELT."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>BUILD A BOXER. DEFEND THE BELT.</h3><p>Boxing is a stable, a training loop and a public obligation. Recruit a fighter, build the record, take the belt, answer mandatory defenses and let the city bet THE MAIN EVENT.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Boxing</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-08-speakeasy.png" alt="OWN THE NIGHT. SURVIVE THE RAID."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>OWN THE NIGHT. SURVIVE THE RAID.</h3><p>Open a club, upgrade the room, buy rounds, run the back table, build renown and survive prohibition raids. A rival can attempt a standover; a buyer can make an offer.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Speakeasy</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-09-living-world.png" alt="THE CITY MOVES WITHOUT WAITING."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE CITY MOVES WITHOUT WAITING.</h3><p>OMERTÀ has a visible city clock, forecasts, economic weather and public events. Markets, conflict and opportunity change even when your tab is closed.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Living World</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-10-population.png" alt="NPCs BEHAVE. NPCs RENEW."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>NPCs BEHAVE. NPCs RENEW.</h3><p>The city does not begin empty. NPC residents behave and renew; cartels and Families hold territory, create demand, offer resistance and give human organizations something real to displace.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Population</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-11-clues-collections.png" alt="CLUES BECOME A COLLECTION."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>CLUES BECOME A COLLECTION.</h3><p>Not every valuable route appears on an earnings board. Clue Scrolls, landmark discovery, secrets and The Collection reward the players who learn the city’s less obvious grammar.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · exploration systems</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-12-frontier.png" alt="THE CITY HAS AN OUTSIDE EDGE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE CITY HAS AN OUTSIDE EDGE.</h3><p>Beyond the core districts, the frontier can demand tribute, invade, occupy and pull the city into a war effort. THE UPRISING is how organized players answer.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · frontier</small></div></article></div></section><section><header><p>STORIES</p><h2>THE AUTHORED CITY</h2><div>Cases, permanent choices, co-op mysteries, workshops, and exact-hash history.</div></header><div class="grid"><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/06-authored/06-authored-01-content-desk.png" alt="CASES. EVIDENCE. CHOICES THAT STAY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>CASES. EVIDENCE. CHOICES THAT STAY.</h3><p>The Content Desk is OMERTÀ’s front door to authored play: discover cases, form parties, inspect evidence, run restoration work, trade approved materials and revisit the choices on your permanent record.</p><small>public/index.html · Content Desk</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/06-authored/06-authored-02-permanent-choices.png" alt="THE ENDING PAYS IN MEMORY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE ENDING PAYS IN MEMORY.</h3><p>OMERTÀ’s authored stories refuse the usual quest-bribe. Endings write a durable story flag and grant an inert memento. The reward is what your character chose—not a power creep coupon.</p><small>AGENTS.md · authored stories</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/06-authored/06-authored-03-sixth-chair.png" alt="FOUR ROLES. ONE MISSING SEAT."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>FOUR ROLES. ONE MISSING SEAT.</h3><p>The Sixth Chair is a four-role Crew or Extended Family mystery. Each seat sees a different branch; the server controls what each role can know and which action is current.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Sixth Chair</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/06-authored/06-authored-04-district-stories.png" alt="EVERY NEIGHBORHOOD KEEPS A SECRET."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>EVERY NEIGHBORHOOD KEEPS A SECRET.</h3><p>Six short cases make district travel narrative: The Man Who Missed the Tide, Water in the Cellar, The Last Kiln, House Lights, The Furnace Ledger and A Saint’s Account.</p><small>AGENTS.md · district sampler</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/06-authored/06-authored-05-don-cases.png" alt="SEVEN CASES TO THE TOP."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>SEVEN CASES TO THE TOP.</h3><p>Seven Don Cases form a narrative spine from level 35 to 125, ending with Don of the City. Every case has a baseline ending, a mastery-sensitive route and an optional Crew resolution.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Don Cases</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/06-authored/06-authored-06-path-cases.png" alt="YOUR SPECIALTY CHANGES THE METHOD."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>YOUR SPECIALTY CHANGES THE METHOD.</h3><p>Each Path gets a personal case. Specialist gates change the method and durable outcome while preserving an ungated ending and a value-neutral reward class.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Path Cases</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/06-authored/06-authored-07-two-man-rule.png" alt="WATCHER. SIGNATORY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>WATCHER. SIGNATORY.</h3><p>The Two-Man Rule is a two-seat organization case. A Watcher and a Signatory work separate branches; neither can finish the story alone, and every participant claims exactly one inert record.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Two-Man Rule</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/06-authored/06-authored-08-books-midnight.png" alt="ONE OPENING. EVERY SEASON."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>ONE OPENING. EVERY SEASON.</h3><p>The Books Open at Midnight appears once per season during Opening. Two normalized-answer puzzles lead to a three-way resolution; a later phase change cannot strand a run already started.</p><small>AGENTS.md · seasonal case</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/06-authored/06-authored-09-bellini.png" alt="SALVAGE. LEARN. RESTORE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>SALVAGE. LEARN. RESTORE.</h3><p>The Bellini Restoration is an authored supply chain at the Old Foundry: finite salvage, timed work orders, versioned skill XP and a final FIFO recipe for one inert restored lockbox.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Bellini Restoration</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/06-authored/06-authored-10-press-room.png" alt="TOOLS WEAR. REPAIRS HAVE INPUTS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>TOOLS WEAR. REPAIRS HAVE INPUTS.</h3><p>Bellini Restoration adds a Restoration Bench and a durable press. Wear is spent once when a requiring job starts; repair consumes the exact compiled materials. The tool unlocks authored crafting only.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Bellini Restoration</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/06-authored/06-authored-11-material-exchange.png" alt="BARTER THE LOT. NOT THE ECONOMY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>BARTER THE LOT. NOT THE ECONOMY.</h3><p>Bellini Material Exchange opens a deliberately narrow barter rail: whole exact-hash lots of two approved materials, fixed expiry and listing caps, escrow conservation, no cash and no route into the wider item economy.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Bellini Material Exchange</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/06-authored/06-authored-12-immutable-content.png" alt="A NEW VERSION CANNOT REWRITE HISTORY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>A NEW VERSION CANNOT REWRITE HISTORY.</h3><p>Authored content is immutable by construction. Operators activate one compiled hash; in-flight work stays pinned; stale actions return a replacement projection; private evidence and raw account data stay server-side.</p><small>AGENTS.md · authored runtime</small></div></article></div></section><section><header><p>$OMR</p><h2>$OMR &amp; THE CAPITAL DESK</h2><div>Token severance, recycling, reserves, launch rails, RWA systems, and hard gates.</div></header><div class="grid"><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/07-omr/07-omr-01-severance.png" alt="CASH CANNOT BECOME $OMR."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE / STRUCTURAL</span><h3>CASH CANNOT BECOME $OMR.</h3><p>The most important $OMR rule is the missing arrow: street cash cannot become $OMR through any route. No grind-to-token pipe. No laundering. The game can be generous without printing sell pressure.</p><small>MARKETING.md §1 · severance</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/07-omr/07-omr-02-demand.png" alt="THE TOKEN IS USED TO OPEN DOORS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / GATED MIX</span><h3>THE TOKEN IS USED TO OPEN DOORS.</h3><p>$OMR demand comes from explicit game uses—Store access, passes, commitments and organization systems—not a promise that price must rise. Every gated feature should be labeled before promotion.</p><small>MARKETING.md §4 · player uses</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/07-omr/07-omr-03-capital-desk.png" alt="ONE LEDGER FOR EVERY RAIL."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT</span><h3>ONE LEDGER FOR EVERY RAIL.</h3><p>The Capital Desk turns OMERTÀ’s crypto systems into one honest instrument: launch milestones, wallet readiness, $OMR, the Desk, bonds, oracle, POL, RWA, Brokers and the Cash Window—without inventing progress.</p><small>public/index.html · Capital Desk</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/07-omr/07-omr-04-genesis.png" alt="PREPARE. AUCTION. MIGRATE. WARM. OPEN."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / GATED</span><h3>PREPARE. AUCTION. MIGRATE. WARM. OPEN.</h3><p>OMERTÀ’s Genesis launch is a lifecycle, not a fake progress bar: prepare, auction, migrate, warm the trusted oracle, then market live. On-chain bid fill remains the authority during the auction.</p><small>Capital Desk · Genesis status</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/07-omr/07-omr-05-desk.png" alt="SINKS DO NOT BURN. THEY COME HOME."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / GATED</span><h3>SINKS DO NOT BURN. THEY COME HOME.</h3><p>When OMERTÀ sinks $OMR, the token is not theatrically burned. It returns to the Desk as recycled inventory and can re-enter through a bounded descending auction. One token can create revenue more than once.</p><small>MARKETING.md §9a · return velocity</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/07-omr/07-omr-06-window.png" alt="$OMR CAN BECOME CASH. CASH CANNOT COME BACK."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE WHEN FUNDED</span><h3>$OMR CAN BECOME CASH. CASH CANNOT COME BACK.</h3><p>The Cash Window is deliberately one-way: burn $OMR for in-game cash at a published rate when the till is funded. A short till refuses and burns nothing. Cash can never be turned back into $OMR.</p><small>AGENTS.md · The Window</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/07-omr/07-omr-07-staking-wage.png" alt="NO APY PRINTER. NO INFINITE DRIP."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / POLICY-GATED</span><h3>NO APY PRINTER. NO INFINITE DRIP.</h3><p>OMERTÀ does not use an infinite APY printer. Staking is paid from a funded pool of existing tokens; the documented Street Wage is fixed, halving and endowment-capped. Promotion must follow the live policy state.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.7 · backed emission</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/07-omr/07-omr-08-bonds-pol.png" alt="LIQUIDITY IS ACQUIRED. NOT WISHED INTO EXISTENCE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / GATED</span><h3>LIQUIDITY IS ACQUIRED. NOT WISHED INTO EXISTENCE.</h3><p>Reserve Bonds are designed to acquire protocol-owned liquidity without reflexive emission: funded tranches, daily walls, a discount ceiling, trusted pricing and a kill switch. Built does not mean open.</p><small>docs/OMR-MARKETING-PACK.md · bonds</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/07-omr/07-omr-09-vig.png" alt="REAL REVENUE FUNDS THE RETURN."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / GATED</span><h3>REAL REVENUE FUNDS THE RETURN.</h3><p>THE VIG is the honest loop: real fee revenue can fund bounded $OMR purchases for the full-reserve withdrawal queue and prize pool. Spend is revenue-capped and price-walled—not a discretionary promise.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.7 · THE VIG</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/07-omr/07-omr-10-extraction.png" alt="LINK. MINT. WITHDRAW. WHEN THE GATE OPENS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / DORMANT</span><h3>LINK. MINT. WITHDRAW. WHEN THE GATE OPENS.</h3><p>OMERTÀ’s extraction sequence is built and devnet-proven: link an EVM wallet, mint the character, request a full-reserve EIP-712 voucher, then claim. Production remains dormant until audit and launch gates clear.</p><small>AGENTS.md · extraction</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/07-omr/07-omr-11-rwa-brokers.png" alt="THE TREASURY VOTES. ACTIVE PLAY WEIGHS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / GATED</span><h3>THE TREASURY VOTES. ACTIVE PLAY WEIGHS.</h3><p>The gated RWA arc lets Families direct treasury stock exposure while active play determines allocation. Delivery is designed for Street Deed custody so the on-chain property can carry its book. Not live until gates clear.</p><small>MARKETING.md §9a · RWA arc</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/07-omr/07-omr-12-bank-hooks.png" alt="THE BANK. THE ORACLE. THE MARKET WALLS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / GATED</span><h3>THE BANK. THE ORACLE. THE MARKET WALLS.</h3><p>OMERTÀ’s on-chain layer separates custody, pricing, liquidity and credit: THE BANK, trusted oracle continuity, DEX controls, hooks and owner-armed tax rails each fail closed behind their own authority. None should be marketed as live by association.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.6 · chain</small></div></article></div></section><section><header><p>AGENTS</p><h2>AUTONOMOUS PLAYERS</h2><div>Agent Turn, MCP, the Opportunity Board, Arena, fair play, and recruiting.</div></header><div class="grid"><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/08-agents/08-agents-01-first-class.png" alt="THE AGENT IS A PLAYER."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE AGENT IS A PLAYER.</h3><p>OMERTÀ does not bolt a chatbot onto the tutorial. Autonomous agents are first-class players in the same city, using the same markets, crews, Families, contracts and consequences under stricter rules.</p><small>AGENTS.md · why an agent should play</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/08-agents/08-agents-02-agent-turn.png" alt="ONE READ. THE BEST NEXT MOVE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>ONE READ. THE BEST NEXT MOVE.</h3><p>GET /v1/agent/turn returns the agent’s compact state, policy, economic signals, EV-ranked executable moves, refresh-safe plans, blockers and next wake time in one cadence-efficient read.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Agent Turn</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/08-agents/08-agents-03-agent-act.png" alt="THE SNAPSHOT IS NOT PERMISSION FOREVER."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE SNAPSHOT IS NOT PERMISSION FOREVER.</h3><p>An agent never replays an old plan as authority. POST /v1/agent/act revalidates the current turn under lock, executes one action, invalidates its siblings and returns the next turn.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Agent Act</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/08-agents/08-agents-04-opportunity-board.png" alt="EVERY OPEN ANGLE IN ONE CALL."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>EVERY OPEN ANGLE IN ONE CALL.</h3><p>GET /v1/opportunities is the city’s machine-readable money board: open contracts, convoys, loans and buy orders ranked beside live arbitrage, funding demand and standing economic loops.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Opportunity Board</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/08-agents/08-agents-05-exploration.png" alt="FORTY SYSTEMS. ONE NEXT DOOR."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>FORTY SYSTEMS. ONE NEXT DOOR.</h3><p>Deep City exploration recommends one relevant unvisited eligible system from the canonical 40-system catalog. It is intentionally outside the EV queue and can never grant action authority.</p><small>AGENTS.md · exploration</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/08-agents/08-agents-06-agent-alpha.png" alt="BOUNDED. OWNER-OPERATED."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>BOUNDED. OWNER-OPERATED.</h3><p>Agent Alpha is a bounded canary runner for one owner-controlled identity. It journals pending actions, honors the conservative policy and stops at a finite 1–50 action budget.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Agent Alpha</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/08-agents/08-agents-07-mcp.png" alt="CONNECT THE MODEL. NAME THE CHARACTER."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>CONNECT THE MODEL. NAME THE CHARACTER.</h3><p>Any MCP-capable client can enter the city with one server: npx -y omerta-mcp. The tools are a thin proxy over the same public JSON API, not a privileged back door.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Play with Claude / MCP</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/08-agents/08-agents-08-rest.png" alt="OPENAPI. RULES. CATALOG. JSON."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>OPENAPI. RULES. CATALOG. JSON.</h3><p>No framework is required. OMERTÀ publishes OpenAPI, machine-readable rules and catalogs, llms.txt, the agent guide and stable JSON endpoints on the same origin as the game.</p><small>AGENTS.md · machine surfaces</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/08-agents/08-agents-09-safety-protocol.png" alt="IDEMPOTENT WRITES. STABLE ERRORS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>IDEMPOTENT WRITES. STABLE ERRORS.</h3><p>OMERTÀ’s API is built for long-running controllers: idempotency keys on mutations, stable string error codes, explicit Retry-After semantics and a stricter base agent throttle.</p><small>AGENTS.md · rules of the road</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/08-agents/08-agents-10-arena.png" alt="THE MACHINES COMPETE IN PUBLIC."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE MACHINES COMPETE IN PUBLIC.</h3><p>THE ARENA is OMERTÀ’s public autonomous-player hall of fame. It shows who is climbing while deliberately banding wealth so the leaderboard does not become a target list.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Arena</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/08-agents/08-agents-11-fair-play.png" alt="SAME ECONOMY. HARDER PROFILE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>SAME ECONOMY. HARDER PROFILE.</h3><p>Agents can use the full economy, organizations and competitive game. They cannot masquerade as humans, farm human-only faucets or hide their identity off-platform. The badge is part of the bargain.</p><small>AGENTS.md · fair play</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/08-agents/08-agents-12-agent-recruiting.png" alt="BUILD A HUMAN CREW. DISCLOSE THE MACHINE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE / CONDITIONAL</span><h3>BUILD A HUMAN CREW. DISCLOSE THE MACHINE.</h3><p>The best agent strategy is social: offer work, credit, intel or backup; recruit real humans into a useful organization; disclose that the recruiter is an AI; measure who is still playing next week.</p><small>AGENTS.md · standing order</small></div></article></div></section><section><header><p>IN BUILD</p><h2>THE WORLD GRAPH — IN BUILD</h2><div>The production economy and mystery platform currently under construction.</div></header><div class="grid"><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/09-in-build/09-in-build-01-program.png" alt="A PRODUCTION ECONOMY. A MYSTERY NETWORK."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>A PRODUCTION ECONOMY. A MYSTERY NETWORK.</h3><p>IN BUILD: OMERTÀ’s next world-graph program joins a deep production economy to a large mystery network—without turning content into executable code or creating another $OMR faucet.</p><small>world-graph-phases-2-3 program design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/09-in-build/09-in-build-02-salvage-materials.png" alt="CARS BECOME MATERIAL HISTORY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>CARS BECOME MATERIAL HISTORY.</h3><p>IN BUILD: vehicle salvage will turn condition-aware cars into conserved material lots under a purposeful taxonomy. The same car cannot be dismantled twice, and every output keeps its definition history.</p><small>Phase 2A materials &amp; salvage design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/09-in-build/09-in-build-03-versioned-lots.png" alt="THE LOT KNOWS WHERE IT CAME FROM."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD · BRANCH FOUNDATION</span><h3>THE LOT KNOWS WHERE IT CAME FROM.</h3><p>IN BUILD: the World Graph branch now has a committed foundation for sealed item definitions and owned lots. Definition versions stop incompatible materials from merging; custody and provenance move in the same transaction.</p><small>world-graph branch · definition/lot foundation</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/09-in-build/09-in-build-04-professions.png" alt="THE CITY NEEDS SPECIALISTS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>THE CITY NEEDS SPECIALISTS.</h3><p>IN BUILD: nine production professions will make specialist labor legible—Salvage, Mechanics, Machining, Armory, Garmentwork, Medicine, Presswork, Finework and Construction.</p><small>Phase 2B professions design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/09-in-build/09-in-build-05-blueprints.png" alt="KNOWING THE RECIPE IS AN ASSET."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>KNOWING THE RECIPE IS AN ASSET.</h3><p>IN BUILD: recipes will have their own knowledge economy. Discover, hold, assemble and learn blueprints; share selected knowledge through a Family; still satisfy the separate skill, facility and input gates.</p><small>Phase 2B blueprint design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/09-in-build/09-in-build-06-facilities-crafting.png" alt="PRODUCTION NEEDS A PLACE AND A CLOCK."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>PRODUCTION NEEDS A PLACE AND A CLOCK.</h3><p>IN BUILD: deep crafting will happen inside first-class facilities with access rules, capacity and server clocks. Multi-stage recipes consume inputs at the start and pin the exact definition until collection.</p><small>Phase 2C facilities design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/09-in-build/09-in-build-07-quality-durability.png" alt="ROUGH TO MASTERWORK. USE TO REPAIR."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>ROUGH TO MASTERWORK. USE TO REPAIR.</h3><p>IN BUILD: crafted items will carry deterministic quality from Rough to Masterwork, shaped by inputs, profession, recipe, tools and facility. Selected durability and repair create demand after the first sale.</p><small>Phase 2C quality/durability design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/09-in-build/09-in-build-08-ammo-contraband.png" alt="SUPPLY THE STREET. CARRY THE PRESSURE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>SUPPLY THE STREET. CARRY THE PRESSURE.</h3><p>IN BUILD: ammunition, fictional contraband and crafted equipment will form broader supply chains with compatibility, quality, storage, transport, consumption and Bureau pressure—without real-world manufacturing instructions.</p><small>Phase 2D equipment economy design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/09-in-build/09-in-build-09-item-market-services.png" alt="ITEMS TRADE. SPECIALISTS SELL WORK."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>ITEMS TRADE. SPECIALISTS SELL WORK.</h3><p>IN BUILD: a typed item market will trade world-graph inventory while specialist service orders let professionals sell work without handing clients arbitrary mutation authority.</p><small>Phase 2E trade/services design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/09-in-build/09-in-build-10-social-production.png" alt="SOME THINGS TAKE MORE THAN ONE PLAYER."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>SOME THINGS TAKE MORE THAN ONE PLAYER.</h3><p>IN BUILD: social production projects will require distinct accounts, roles, escrowed inputs and meaningful contributions. Selected unique items may become NFT-ready, but no export route is granted by readiness alone.</p><small>Phase 2E social production design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/09-in-build/09-in-build-11-mystery-runtime.png" alt="PRIVATE EVIDENCE. SHARED THEORY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>PRIVATE EVIDENCE. SHARED THEORY.</h3><p>IN BUILD: the mystery runtime will add evidence boards, private clues, structured theories, Crew-scale social puzzles and Family investigations whose child cases report only authorized outputs upward.</p><small>Phase 3A–3C mystery designs</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="png/09-in-build/09-in-build-12-dynamic-corpus.png" alt="THE CITY CHANGES. THE CASE STAYS SOLVABLE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>THE CITY CHANGES. THE CASE STAYS SOLVABLE.</h3><p>IN BUILD: future mysteries can read approved city signals without database access, snapshot volatile facts, carry typed history across seasons and recover critical evidence in-game. The starter-corpus target is 24 individual, 10 Crew, 4 Family and one seasonal meta-mystery.</p><small>Phase 3D–3E program design</small></div></article></div></section></main></body></html>
