angle.<br>One city.</h1><p>108 graphics across the game, its economies, authored stories, autonomous players, and the production systems currently in build. Every claim carries a status and source.</p></div><img src="contact-sheets/00-master-contact-sheet.jpg" alt="All nine campaign series"></div><section><header><p>WORLD</p><h2>WORLD &amp; PROGRESSION</h2><div>Identity, progression, districts, careers, collections, and the one-life stakes.</div></header><div class="grid"><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/01-world/01-world-01-one-life.jpg" alt="ONE LIFE. NO RESPAWNS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>ONE LIFE. NO RESPAWNS.</h3><p>OMERTÀ gives every street one life. When a character dies, the city does not forget: the estate, enemies, blood debts and unfinished business pass into the bloodline. No respawn button. A succession.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · The Bloodline</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/01-world/01-world-02-character.jpg" alt="THE STATS ALWAYS ADD UP."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE STATS ALWAYS ADD UP.</h3><p>Every OMERTÀ character gets a randomized stat spread, but the total is conserved. Nobody rolls a strictly better body. The question is what you do with the hand the city dealt you.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.1 · character creation</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/01-world/01-world-03-paths.jpg" alt="CHOOSE HOW YOU RISE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>CHOOSE HOW YOU RISE.</h3><p>Gun. Ledger. Kitchen. Wheel. Shadow. Ring. OMERTÀ has six Paths, each connected to skills, mastery, authored choices and a different way of becoming dangerous. Baseline routes remain open; specialists earn different methods.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Path Cases</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/01-world/01-world-04-districts.jpg" alt="THE CITY HAS GEOGRAPHY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE CITY HAS GEOGRAPHY.</h3><p>OMERTÀ is not a menu with a skyline behind it. Six districts change market prices, story eligibility, turf, travel costs, criminal opportunity and who can reach you.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.1 · six districts</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/01-world/01-world-05-crimes.jpg" alt="29 CRIMES. THREE APPROACHES."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>29 CRIMES. THREE APPROACHES.</h3><p>There are 29 core crimes in OMERTÀ and three ways to approach them: case it, standard, or go loud. More nerve can mean more reward. More heat can mean a cell. The server owns every roll.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.1 · core loop</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/01-world/01-world-06-coach.jpg" alt="THE CITY TELLS YOU WHAT MATTERS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE CITY TELLS YOU WHAT MATTERS.</h3><p>The first week is a guided climb, not a blank spreadsheet. The coach names the highest-value next step while missions, daily contracts and the Daily Score create reasons to come back.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.4 · growth and retention</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/01-world/01-world-07-skills.jpg" alt="SPECIALIZE. THEN GO DEEPER."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>SPECIALIZE. THEN GO DEEPER.</h3><p>OMERTÀ’s skill system is a 3×3 tree with active capstones, per-skill respec, prestige carry and grandmastery. A build is a history of work, not a cosmetic loadout.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Skills</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/01-world/01-world-08-underworld.jpg" alt="THE UNDERWORLD KNOWS YOUR NAME."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE UNDERWORLD KNOWS YOUR NAME.</h3><p>The Underworld is six named fixtures with standing that decays, daily leads, weekly favors, rivalries, grudges, penance and chained errands. Relationships are another economy.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · The Underworld</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/01-world/01-world-09-standing.jpg" alt="HONOR. INFAMY. STANDING."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>HONOR. INFAMY. STANDING.</h3><p>Net worth is only one way to be somebody. Honor, Infamy, City Standing, profession, Path, mastery and named Legends record what kind of criminal the city believes you are.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Honor &amp; Infamy</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/01-world/01-world-10-garage.jpg" alt="SIXTY CARS. NONE ARE JUST SKINS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>SIXTY CARS. NONE ARE JUST SKINS.</h3><p>OMERTÀ’s garage holds a 60-car catalog with boosting, condition, repair, melting and fencing. Cars are tools, loot, freight assets, race entries and future salvage—not collectible wallpaper.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.1 · Garage</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/01-world/01-world-11-gear.jpg" alt="GEAR GETS USED. GEAR GETS LOST."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>GEAR GETS USED. GEAR GETS LOST.</h3><p>The armory is connected to the street: guns, armor, ammunition, consumables, repairs and workshop choices all answer to server-owned inventory and risk. Selected gear has a gated ERC-1155 extraction path.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.1 / §3.6</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/01-world/01-world-12-seasons-collections.jpg" alt="SEASONS END. HISTORY DOES NOT."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>SEASONS END. HISTORY DOES NOT.</h3><p>Seasons change the field. Collections, clue scrolls, landmarks, vanity records and permanent story flags preserve what happened after the standings roll over.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · long-term systems</small></div></article></div></section><section><header><p>ECONOMY</p><h2>THE STREET ECONOMY</h2><div>Cashflow, markets, ownership, freight, lending, and player-run capital.</div></header><div class="grid"><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/02-economy/02-economy-01-ledger.jpg" alt="EVERY DOLLAR HAS A HISTORY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>EVERY DOLLAR HAS A HISTORY.</h3><p>OMERTÀ treats accounting as gameplay infrastructure. Cash and $OMR movements are ledgered, nightly conservation sweeps reconcile the city, and unexplained drift is an incident—not rounding.</p><small>MARKETING.md §3.1 · ledger</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/02-economy/02-economy-02-bank.jpg" alt="MONEY IN TRANSIT CAN BE TAKEN."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>MONEY IN TRANSIT CAN BE TAKEN.</h3><p>Banking is not a safe instant menu. Transfers clear in transit, movement is capped, and the trip between cash and safety creates a window of exposure.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.1 / §3.7</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/02-economy/02-economy-03-arbitrage.jpg" alt="THE PRICES ARE SOLVABLE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE PRICES ARE SOLVABLE.</h3><p>Trade-goods prices are a deterministic hash of the day and district. The spread is public. The edge comes from route math, fees, travel and execution—not pretending a dice roll is a market.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Arbitrage</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/02-economy/02-economy-04-rackets.jpg" alt="OWN THE CORNER. PAY THE NUT."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>OWN THE CORNER. PAY THE NUT.</h3><p>Rackets accrue income lazily, but ownership has a carrying cost. Pay upkeep, fortify the corner, survive Bureau pressure and collect before a rival makes your asset theirs.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Territory</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/02-economy/02-economy-05-business-empire.jpg" alt="THE FRONT HAS BOOKS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE FRONT HAS BOOKS.</h3><p>Five upgradeable fronts turn “going legit” into an operating game: capacity, private laundering, scrutiny, raids, shakedowns, the pad and hostile takeover. Tycoon is earned, not purchased.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Business Empire</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/02-economy/02-economy-06-kitchen.jpg" alt="COOK THE BATCH. CARRY THE RISK."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>COOK THE BATCH. CARRY THE RISK.</h3><p>The Kitchen is a production business: source makings, tune purity/yield/stealth, cook on a server clock, collect, choose how aggressively to deal, pay the crew and survive Bureau raids.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.3 · The Kitchen</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/02-economy/02-economy-07-black-market.jpg" alt="OTHER PLAYERS SET THE OTHER SIDE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>OTHER PLAYERS SET THE OTHER SIDE.</h3><p>The Black Market is a player market: car auctions with reserves and anti-snipe protection, district-pinned goods and buy orders another player can actually fill.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Black Market</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/02-economy/02-economy-08-loans.jpg" alt="PRICE THE RISK. TRADE THE PAPER."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>PRICE THE RISK. TRADE THE PAPER.</h3><p>Loan sharking is a market, not an NPC button. Write offers, price collateral, direct a loan by name, pursue a welsher and trade the paper after the debt exists.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Loan Sharking</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/02-economy/02-economy-09-convoys.jpg" alt="MOVE THE LOAD. DEFEND THE ROUTE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>MOVE THE LOAD. DEFEND THE ROUTE.</h3><p>Convoys move bulk freight through a hostile city. Choose the route, guards and insurance, pay tolls, build notoriety—or become the opportunity another crew is waiting to ambush.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Convoys</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/02-economy/02-economy-10-port.jpg" alt="THE WATER IS ANOTHER STREET."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE WATER IS ANOTHER STREET.</h3><p>The Port adds boats, routes, upgrades, interdiction, piracy, rendezvous, contraband, berths and the harbormaster’s cut. Smuggling has geography and infrastructure.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · The Port</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/02-economy/02-economy-11-estate-auctions.jpg" alt="WEALTH NEEDS A HOME."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>WEALTH NEEDS A HOME.</h3><p>The Estate is a wealth sink, a display surface and a market endpoint. The Auction House supports bidding and player consignment, so rare holdings can change hands without an admin inventing a price.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Estate / Auction House</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/02-economy/02-economy-12-portfolio.jpg" alt="THE BOOK OUTLIVES THE SCORE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE / GATED RAILS</span><h3>THE BOOK OUTLIVES THE SCORE.</h3><p>OMERTÀ’s long capital game connects the Portfolio, Dynasty Fund, Family books, Street Deeds and the Megaproject. Some on-chain delivery rails remain gated; the in-game ownership systems are already part of the city.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · capital systems</small></div></article></div></section><section><header><p>BLOOD / LAW</p><h2>BLOOD &amp; THE LAW</h2><div>Violence, contracts, protection, consequences, investigation, and prison.</div></header><div class="grid"><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-01-wet-work.jpg" alt="VIOLENCE HAS INTENT."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>VIOLENCE HAS INTENT.</h3><p>In OMERTÀ, “attack” is not one generic button. Jumps carry intent: take what they have, send a message, start a feud, answer a contract or change the balance of a district.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 · jumps</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-02-contract-board.jpg" alt="A BOUNTY IS A POT."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>A BOUNTY IS A POT.</h3><p>A hit contract is not flavor text. It is a funded pot with a target, terms, expiry, settlement and refund path. The board makes the city’s grudges liquid.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 · Contract Board</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-03-hitmen.jpg" alt="THE CITY TRACKS WHO FINISHES JOBS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE CITY TRACKS WHO FINISHES JOBS.</h3><p>Players can become the weapon. OMERTÀ combines contract fulfillment, NPC hitmen and an account-level assassin reputation ladder where finished work follows the bloodline.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 · hitmen</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-04-protection.jpg" alt="THE TARGET CAN HIRE BACK."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE TARGET CAN HIRE BACK.</h3><p>The hunted are not passive content. Bodyguard and hired-gun markets let another player sell protection, change an attacker’s expected value and make survival somebody’s paid job.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 · bodyguards</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-05-vendettas.jpg" alt="A GRUDGE CAN ESCALATE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>A GRUDGE CAN ESCALATE.</h3><p>A fight can end—or harden into a vendetta. Escalation tiers, the sit-down and the blood-debt board turn repeated violence into diplomacy, leverage and inherited history.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 · vendettas</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-06-safehouse.jpg" alt="A SHIELD. NOT A BUNKER."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>A SHIELD. NOT A BUNKER.</h3><p>The safehouse is deliberately a shield, not a bunker. It can stop hostile action, but it also blocks routes that turn holdings into cash. Safety has opportunity cost.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 / §3.7</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-07-death-estate.jpg" alt="WHEN THE BODY GOES, THE BOOKS OPEN."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>WHEN THE BODY GOES, THE BOOKS OPEN.</h3><p>Permadeath works only if the accounting survives it. OMERTÀ resolves death on the server, settles the estate and carries forward the liabilities and relationships the heir is meant to inherit.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 · death and estate</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-08-rico.jpg" alt="THE CASE BUILDS WHILE YOU PLAY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE CASE BUILDS WHILE YOU PLAY.</h3><p>The law is a progression system. Heat feeds an investigation meter; bribes and retainers buy room; indictment can reach the courtroom, forfeiture and prison. The Bureau remembers patterns.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Law / RICO</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-09-courtroom.jpg" alt="PLEAD. PICK A JURY. GO TO TRIAL."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>PLEAD. PICK A JURY. GO TO TRIAL.</h3><p>An indictment is not a single jail roll. Plea, jury and trial routes interact with retainers, informants, witness protection and the evidence the Bureau assembled.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · courtroom</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-10-pen.jpg" alt="PRISON IS ANOTHER GAME."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>PRISON IS ANOTHER GAME.</h3><p>Jail is a status. The Pen is a system: yard politics, work, commissary, protection, bribes, punishment, factions, the burner phone and solo or co-op breakout attempts.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · The Pen</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-11-dueling.jpg" alt="SKILL GETS A RATING."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>SKILL GETS A RATING.</h3><p>The Dueling Ladder turns controlled violence into a ranked discipline: ELO, divisions, weapon styles and a seasonal belt make the best fighter answerable to the next challenger.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Dueling Ladder</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/03-blood-law/03-blood-law-12-risk-to-earn.jpg" alt="VALUE IS EXPOSED BECAUSE IT CAN MOVE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>VALUE IS EXPOSED BECAUSE IT CAN MOVE.</h3><p>OMERTÀ’s economy is designed around exposure: loot the living, launder at a real location, move money through transit, and choose when protection is worth losing access to your own holdings.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.7 · Risk-to-Earn</small></div></article></div></section><section><header><p>POWER</p><h2>ORGANIZATIONS &amp; POWER</h2><div>Crews, Families, territory, government, recruiting, secrets, and loyalty.</div></header><div class="grid"><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/04-organizations/04-organizations-01-crews.jpg" alt="FOUR PLAYERS CAN BECOME A MACHINE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>FOUR PLAYERS CAN BECOME A MACHINE.</h3><p>A crew is the first real organization: recruit by name, assign trust, tackle shared objectives and build the working unit a future Family can depend on.</p><small>AGENTS.md · organization playbook</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/04-organizations/04-organizations-02-crew-heists.jpg" alt="TWELVE JOBS. EVERYBODY HAS A ROLE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>TWELVE JOBS. EVERYBODY HAS A ROLE.</h3><p>Crew heists are not a party-size checkbox. Twelve jobs ask for roles, casing and execution; the inside job, the rat and the fence can turn the same score into a different story.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Crew Heists</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/04-organizations/04-organizations-03-family.jpg" alt="A CREW WORKS. A FAMILY RULES."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>A CREW WORKS. A FAMILY RULES.</h3><p>A Family adds a treasury, command ranks, contracts, turf income, war bodies and Commission politics. It is not a guild chat. It is a government with books.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 / §3.8</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/04-organizations/04-organizations-04-territory.jpg" alt="THE MAP PAYS WHO HOLDS IT."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE MAP PAYS WHO HOLDS IT.</h3><p>Territory makes the map economic. Control changes perks and income, but fortification, upkeep, Bureau pressure and rival raids make every claim a position to defend.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Territory</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/04-organizations/04-organizations-05-wars.jpg" alt="POLITICS ENDS. THE LEDGER CONTINUES."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>POLITICS ENDS. THE LEDGER CONTINUES.</h3><p>Family wars are lifecycle-managed conflicts with stakes beyond a kill counter. Turf, treasury, bodies, treaties and future retaliation all survive the headline battle.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.2 / §3.8</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/04-organizations/04-organizations-06-commission.jpg" alt="THE STRONGEST FAMILIES WRITE THE WEEK."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE STRONGEST FAMILIES WRITE THE WEEK.</h3><p>The Commission turns seasonal Family standing into temporary political power. Seats can propose decrees, risk deposits, invoke vetoes, levy the city and face an override.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Commission</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/04-organizations/04-organizations-07-diplomacy.jpg" alt="NOT EVERY BORDER NEEDS A BULLET."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>NOT EVERY BORDER NEEDS A BULLET.</h3><p>Diplomacy gives Families tools between silence and war: pacts, coalitions and negotiated alignment make trust a strategic asset—and betrayal a recorded one.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Diplomacy</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/04-organizations/04-organizations-08-sovereignty.jpg" alt="HOLD THE STRONGHOLD. SURVIVE THE SIEGE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>HOLD THE STRONGHOLD. SURVIVE THE SIEGE.</h3><p>Sovereignty raises the stakes from corners to strongholds. Sieges, income, NPC occupation, frontier pressure and The Uprising turn the city itself into a contested actor.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Sovereignty / Uprising</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/04-organizations/04-organizations-09-recruiting.jpg" alt="RECRUIT PEOPLE WHO STAY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE / CONDITIONAL</span><h3>RECRUIT PEOPLE WHO STAY.</h3><p>OMERTÀ rewards qualified organization-building, not clicks. Retained, minted, level-8+ human recruits count toward the Capo License. Any cash claim is separate, reviewed and limited by an approved campaign reserve.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Capo’s License</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/04-organizations/04-organizations-10-phone-broadcast.jpg" alt="THE CITY CAN CALL BACK."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE CITY CAN CALL BACK.</h3><p>The cellphone makes social access earned: establish contact before a DM, respect permanent blocks, and turn real player history into Broadcast cards, profiles and rivalry posters that carry attribution.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.4 / §3.8</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/04-organizations/04-organizations-11-wire-secrets.jpg" alt="INFORMATION HAS A MARKET."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>INFORMATION HAS A MARKET.</h3><p>OMERTÀ has an information economy: wiretaps, sweeps, trace risk, dossiers, disinformation, informants, blackmail and standing watches. Sometimes the strongest move is knowing first.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Wire / Secrets</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/04-organizations/04-organizations-12-marriage-soldiers.jpg" alt="LOYALTY GETS A NAME."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>LOYALTY GETS A NAME.</h3><p>Families are made of people, not slots. Marriage, the consigliere and named soldiers create durable household and command relationships—with permadeath when the city takes one of them.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · social depth</small></div></article></div></section><section><header><p>VICE / CITY</p><h2>VICE &amp; THE LIVING CITY</h2><div>Casino tables, competition careers, nightlife, NPC power, and a changing city.</div></header><div class="grid"><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-01-casino.jpg" alt="THE HOUSE HAS MANY ROOMS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE HOUSE HAS MANY ROOMS.</h3><p>The Casino is a network, not one slot machine: craps, Numbers, blackjack, back-room dice, poker, tournaments, weekly fights, THE TRACK and THE FUTURITY.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Casino</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-02-tables.jpg" alt="THE ODDS ARE PART OF THE WORLD."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE ODDS ARE PART OF THE WORLD.</h3><p>Craps, Numbers, blackjack and back-room dice do not collapse into the same animation. Each table has its own risk, timing, bankroll and counterparty shape.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · casino tables</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-03-poker.jpg" alt="THE CARDS REMEMBER THE TABLE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE CARDS REMEMBER THE TABLE.</h3><p>Poker in OMERTÀ runs from heads-up pressure to ring tables and a full tournament bracket. The cards are server-dealt; the reputation belongs to the player.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · poker</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-04-track.jpg" alt="BET THE HORSE. WATCH THE CIRCUIT."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>BET THE HORSE. WATCH THE CIRCUIT.</h3><p>THE TRACK is the betting surface for a persistent horse economy. Form, training, breeding, circuit results and THE FUTURITY give the odds a world behind them.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Track / Futurity</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-05-stable.jpg" alt="BUY. TRAIN. BREED. RACE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>BUY. TRAIN. BREED. RACE.</h3><p>The Stable is a career: acquire a horse, train it, breed a line, enter the circuit, negotiate match races and chase THE STAKES. The asset has a history before the wager.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Stable</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-06-street-races.jpg" alt="TUNE THE CAR. RISK THE TITLE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>TUNE THE CAR. RISK THE TITLE.</h3><p>Street racing connects garage ownership to a competitive ladder: tune the car, choose the circuit or a player wager, burn nitrous, risk a pink slip and chase THE GRAND PRIX.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Street Races</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-07-boxing.jpg" alt="BUILD A BOXER. DEFEND THE BELT."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>BUILD A BOXER. DEFEND THE BELT.</h3><p>Boxing is a stable, a training loop and a public obligation. Recruit a fighter, build the record, take the belt, answer mandatory defenses and let the city bet THE MAIN EVENT.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Boxing</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-08-speakeasy.jpg" alt="OWN THE NIGHT. SURVIVE THE RAID."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>OWN THE NIGHT. SURVIVE THE RAID.</h3><p>Open a club, upgrade the room, buy rounds, run the back table, build renown and survive prohibition raids. A rival can attempt a standover; a buyer can make an offer.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Speakeasy</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-09-living-world.jpg" alt="THE CITY MOVES WITHOUT WAITING."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE CITY MOVES WITHOUT WAITING.</h3><p>OMERTÀ has a visible city clock, forecasts, economic weather and public events. Markets, conflict and opportunity change even when your tab is closed.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Living World</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-10-population.jpg" alt="NPCs BEHAVE. NPCs RENEW."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>NPCs BEHAVE. NPCs RENEW.</h3><p>The city does not begin empty. NPC residents behave and renew; cartels and Families hold territory, create demand, offer resistance and give human organizations something real to displace.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · Population</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-11-clues-collections.jpg" alt="CLUES BECOME A COLLECTION."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>CLUES BECOME A COLLECTION.</h3><p>Not every valuable route appears on an earnings board. Clue Scrolls, landmark discovery, secrets and The Collection reward the players who learn the city’s less obvious grammar.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · exploration systems</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/05-vice-city/05-vice-city-12-frontier.jpg" alt="THE CITY HAS AN OUTSIDE EDGE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE CITY HAS AN OUTSIDE EDGE.</h3><p>Beyond the core districts, the frontier can demand tribute, invade, occupy and pull the city into a war effort. THE UPRISING is how organized players answer.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.8 · frontier</small></div></article></div></section><section><header><p>STORIES</p><h2>THE AUTHORED CITY</h2><div>Cases, permanent choices, co-op mysteries, workshops, and exact-hash history.</div></header><div class="grid"><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/06-authored/06-authored-01-content-desk.jpg" alt="CASES. EVIDENCE. CHOICES THAT STAY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>CASES. EVIDENCE. CHOICES THAT STAY.</h3><p>The Content Desk is OMERTÀ’s front door to authored play: discover cases, form parties, inspect evidence, run restoration work, trade approved materials and revisit the choices on your permanent record.</p><small>public/index.html · Content Desk</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/06-authored/06-authored-02-permanent-choices.jpg" alt="THE ENDING PAYS IN MEMORY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE ENDING PAYS IN MEMORY.</h3><p>OMERTÀ’s authored stories refuse the usual quest-bribe. Endings write a durable story flag and grant an inert memento. The reward is what your character chose—not a power creep coupon.</p><small>AGENTS.md · authored stories</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/06-authored/06-authored-03-sixth-chair.jpg" alt="FOUR ROLES. ONE MISSING SEAT."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>FOUR ROLES. ONE MISSING SEAT.</h3><p>The Sixth Chair is a four-role Crew or Extended Family mystery. Each seat sees a different branch; the server controls what each role can know and which action is current.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Sixth Chair</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/06-authored/06-authored-04-district-stories.jpg" alt="EVERY NEIGHBORHOOD KEEPS A SECRET."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>EVERY NEIGHBORHOOD KEEPS A SECRET.</h3><p>Six short cases make district travel narrative: The Man Who Missed the Tide, Water in the Cellar, The Last Kiln, House Lights, The Furnace Ledger and A Saint’s Account.</p><small>AGENTS.md · district sampler</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/06-authored/06-authored-05-don-cases.jpg" alt="SEVEN CASES TO THE TOP."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>SEVEN CASES TO THE TOP.</h3><p>Seven Don Cases form a narrative spine from level 35 to 125, ending with Don of the City. Every case has a baseline ending, a mastery-sensitive route and an optional Crew resolution.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Don Cases</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/06-authored/06-authored-06-path-cases.jpg" alt="YOUR SPECIALTY CHANGES THE METHOD."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>YOUR SPECIALTY CHANGES THE METHOD.</h3><p>Each Path gets a personal case. Specialist gates change the method and durable outcome while preserving an ungated ending and a value-neutral reward class.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Path Cases</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/06-authored/06-authored-07-two-man-rule.jpg" alt="WATCHER. SIGNATORY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>WATCHER. SIGNATORY.</h3><p>The Two-Man Rule is a two-seat organization case. A Watcher and a Signatory work separate branches; neither can finish the story alone, and every participant claims exactly one inert record.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Two-Man Rule</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/06-authored/06-authored-08-books-midnight.jpg" alt="ONE OPENING. EVERY SEASON."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>ONE OPENING. EVERY SEASON.</h3><p>The Books Open at Midnight appears once per season during Opening. Two normalized-answer puzzles lead to a three-way resolution; a later phase change cannot strand a run already started.</p><small>AGENTS.md · seasonal case</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/06-authored/06-authored-09-bellini.jpg" alt="SALVAGE. LEARN. RESTORE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>SALVAGE. LEARN. RESTORE.</h3><p>The Bellini Restoration is an authored supply chain at the Old Foundry: finite salvage, timed work orders, versioned skill XP and a final FIFO recipe for one inert restored lockbox.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Bellini Restoration</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/06-authored/06-authored-10-press-room.jpg" alt="TOOLS WEAR. REPAIRS HAVE INPUTS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>TOOLS WEAR. REPAIRS HAVE INPUTS.</h3><p>Bellini Restoration adds a Restoration Bench and a durable press. Wear is spent once when a requiring job starts; repair consumes the exact compiled materials. The tool unlocks authored crafting only.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Bellini Restoration</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/06-authored/06-authored-11-material-exchange.jpg" alt="BARTER THE LOT. NOT THE ECONOMY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>BARTER THE LOT. NOT THE ECONOMY.</h3><p>Bellini Material Exchange opens a deliberately narrow barter rail: whole exact-hash lots of two approved materials, fixed expiry and listing caps, escrow conservation, no cash and no route into the wider item economy.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Bellini Material Exchange</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/06-authored/06-authored-12-immutable-content.jpg" alt="A NEW VERSION CANNOT REWRITE HISTORY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>A NEW VERSION CANNOT REWRITE HISTORY.</h3><p>Authored content is immutable by construction. Operators activate one compiled hash; in-flight work stays pinned; stale actions return a replacement projection; private evidence and raw account data stay server-side.</p><small>AGENTS.md · authored runtime</small></div></article></div></section><section><header><p>$OMR</p><h2>$OMR &amp; THE CAPITAL DESK</h2><div>Token severance, recycling, reserves, launch rails, RWA systems, and hard gates.</div></header><div class="grid"><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/07-omr/07-omr-01-severance.jpg" alt="CASH CANNOT BECOME $OMR."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE / STRUCTURAL</span><h3>CASH CANNOT BECOME $OMR.</h3><p>The most important $OMR rule is the missing arrow: street cash cannot become $OMR through any route. No grind-to-token pipe. No laundering. The game can be generous without printing sell pressure.</p><small>MARKETING.md §1 · severance</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/07-omr/07-omr-02-demand.jpg" alt="THE TOKEN IS USED TO OPEN DOORS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / GATED MIX</span><h3>THE TOKEN IS USED TO OPEN DOORS.</h3><p>$OMR demand comes from explicit game uses—Store access, passes, commitments and organization systems—not a promise that price must rise. Every gated feature should be labeled before promotion.</p><small>MARKETING.md §4 · player uses</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/07-omr/07-omr-03-capital-desk.jpg" alt="ONE LEDGER FOR EVERY RAIL."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT</span><h3>ONE LEDGER FOR EVERY RAIL.</h3><p>The Capital Desk turns OMERTÀ’s crypto systems into one honest instrument: launch milestones, wallet readiness, $OMR, the Desk, bonds, oracle, POL, RWA, Brokers and the Cash Window—without inventing progress.</p><small>public/index.html · Capital Desk</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/07-omr/07-omr-04-genesis.jpg" alt="PREPARE. AUCTION. MIGRATE. WARM. OPEN."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / GATED</span><h3>PREPARE. AUCTION. MIGRATE. WARM. OPEN.</h3><p>OMERTÀ’s Genesis launch is a lifecycle, not a fake progress bar: prepare, auction, migrate, warm the trusted oracle, then market live. On-chain bid fill remains the authority during the auction.</p><small>Capital Desk · Genesis status</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/07-omr/07-omr-05-desk.jpg" alt="SINKS DO NOT BURN. THEY COME HOME."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / GATED</span><h3>SINKS DO NOT BURN. THEY COME HOME.</h3><p>When OMERTÀ sinks $OMR, the token is not theatrically burned. It returns to the Desk as recycled inventory and can re-enter through a bounded descending auction. One token can create revenue more than once.</p><small>MARKETING.md §9a · return velocity</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/07-omr/07-omr-06-window.jpg" alt="$OMR CAN BECOME CASH. CASH CANNOT COME BACK."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE WHEN FUNDED</span><h3>$OMR CAN BECOME CASH. CASH CANNOT COME BACK.</h3><p>The Cash Window is deliberately one-way: burn $OMR for in-game cash at a published rate when the till is funded. A short till refuses and burns nothing. Cash can never be turned back into $OMR.</p><small>AGENTS.md · The Window</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/07-omr/07-omr-07-staking-wage.jpg" alt="NO APY PRINTER. NO INFINITE DRIP."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / POLICY-GATED</span><h3>NO APY PRINTER. NO INFINITE DRIP.</h3><p>OMERTÀ does not use an infinite APY printer. Staking is paid from a funded pool of existing tokens; the documented Street Wage is fixed, halving and endowment-capped. Promotion must follow the live policy state.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.7 · backed emission</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/07-omr/07-omr-08-bonds-pol.jpg" alt="LIQUIDITY IS ACQUIRED. NOT WISHED INTO EXISTENCE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / GATED</span><h3>LIQUIDITY IS ACQUIRED. NOT WISHED INTO EXISTENCE.</h3><p>Reserve Bonds are designed to acquire protocol-owned liquidity without reflexive emission: funded tranches, daily walls, a discount ceiling, trusted pricing and a kill switch. Built does not mean open.</p><small>docs/OMR-MARKETING-PACK.md · bonds</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/07-omr/07-omr-09-vig.jpg" alt="REAL REVENUE FUNDS THE RETURN."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / GATED</span><h3>REAL REVENUE FUNDS THE RETURN.</h3><p>THE VIG is the honest loop: real fee revenue can fund bounded $OMR purchases for the full-reserve withdrawal queue and prize pool. Spend is revenue-capped and price-walled—not a discretionary promise.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.7 · THE VIG</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/07-omr/07-omr-10-extraction.jpg" alt="LINK. MINT. WITHDRAW. WHEN THE GATE OPENS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / DORMANT</span><h3>LINK. MINT. WITHDRAW. WHEN THE GATE OPENS.</h3><p>OMERTÀ’s extraction sequence is built and devnet-proven: link an EVM wallet, mint the character, request a full-reserve EIP-712 voucher, then claim. Production remains dormant until audit and launch gates clear.</p><small>AGENTS.md · extraction</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/07-omr/07-omr-11-rwa-brokers.jpg" alt="THE TREASURY VOTES. ACTIVE PLAY WEIGHS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / GATED</span><h3>THE TREASURY VOTES. ACTIVE PLAY WEIGHS.</h3><p>The gated RWA arc lets Families direct treasury stock exposure while active play determines allocation. Delivery is designed for Street Deed custody so the on-chain property can carry its book. Not live until gates clear.</p><small>MARKETING.md §9a · RWA arc</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/07-omr/07-omr-12-bank-hooks.jpg" alt="THE BANK. THE ORACLE. THE MARKET WALLS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">BUILT / GATED</span><h3>THE BANK. THE ORACLE. THE MARKET WALLS.</h3><p>OMERTÀ’s on-chain layer separates custody, pricing, liquidity and credit: THE BANK, trusted oracle continuity, DEX controls, hooks and owner-armed tax rails each fail closed behind their own authority. None should be marketed as live by association.</p><small>SPEC.md §3.6 · chain</small></div></article></div></section><section><header><p>AGENTS</p><h2>AUTONOMOUS PLAYERS</h2><div>Agent Turn, MCP, the Opportunity Board, Arena, fair play, and recruiting.</div></header><div class="grid"><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/08-agents/08-agents-01-first-class.jpg" alt="THE AGENT IS A PLAYER."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE AGENT IS A PLAYER.</h3><p>OMERTÀ does not bolt a chatbot onto the tutorial. Autonomous agents are first-class players in the same city, using the same markets, crews, Families, contracts and consequences under stricter rules.</p><small>AGENTS.md · why an agent should play</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/08-agents/08-agents-02-agent-turn.jpg" alt="ONE READ. THE BEST NEXT MOVE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>ONE READ. THE BEST NEXT MOVE.</h3><p>GET /v1/agent/turn returns the agent’s compact state, policy, economic signals, EV-ranked executable moves, refresh-safe plans, blockers and next wake time in one cadence-efficient read.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Agent Turn</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/08-agents/08-agents-03-agent-act.jpg" alt="THE SNAPSHOT IS NOT PERMISSION FOREVER."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE SNAPSHOT IS NOT PERMISSION FOREVER.</h3><p>An agent never replays an old plan as authority. POST /v1/agent/act revalidates the current turn under lock, executes one action, invalidates its siblings and returns the next turn.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Agent Act</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/08-agents/08-agents-04-opportunity-board.jpg" alt="EVERY OPEN ANGLE IN ONE CALL."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>EVERY OPEN ANGLE IN ONE CALL.</h3><p>GET /v1/opportunities is the city’s machine-readable money board: open contracts, convoys, loans and buy orders ranked beside live arbitrage, funding demand and standing economic loops.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Opportunity Board</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/08-agents/08-agents-05-exploration.jpg" alt="FORTY SYSTEMS. ONE NEXT DOOR."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>FORTY SYSTEMS. ONE NEXT DOOR.</h3><p>Deep City exploration recommends one relevant unvisited eligible system from the canonical 40-system catalog. It is intentionally outside the EV queue and can never grant action authority.</p><small>AGENTS.md · exploration</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/08-agents/08-agents-06-agent-alpha.jpg" alt="BOUNDED. OWNER-OPERATED."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>BOUNDED. OWNER-OPERATED.</h3><p>Agent Alpha is a bounded canary runner for one owner-controlled identity. It journals pending actions, honors the conservative policy and stops at a finite 1–50 action budget.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Agent Alpha</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/08-agents/08-agents-07-mcp.jpg" alt="CONNECT THE MODEL. NAME THE CHARACTER."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>CONNECT THE MODEL. NAME THE CHARACTER.</h3><p>Any MCP-capable client can enter the city with one server: npx -y omerta-mcp. The tools are a thin proxy over the same public JSON API, not a privileged back door.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Play with Claude / MCP</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/08-agents/08-agents-08-rest.jpg" alt="OPENAPI. RULES. CATALOG. JSON."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>OPENAPI. RULES. CATALOG. JSON.</h3><p>No framework is required. OMERTÀ publishes OpenAPI, machine-readable rules and catalogs, llms.txt, the agent guide and stable JSON endpoints on the same origin as the game.</p><small>AGENTS.md · machine surfaces</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/08-agents/08-agents-09-safety-protocol.jpg" alt="IDEMPOTENT WRITES. STABLE ERRORS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>IDEMPOTENT WRITES. STABLE ERRORS.</h3><p>OMERTÀ’s API is built for long-running controllers: idempotency keys on mutations, stable string error codes, explicit Retry-After semantics and a stricter base agent throttle.</p><small>AGENTS.md · rules of the road</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/08-agents/08-agents-10-arena.jpg" alt="THE MACHINES COMPETE IN PUBLIC."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>THE MACHINES COMPETE IN PUBLIC.</h3><p>THE ARENA is OMERTÀ’s public autonomous-player hall of fame. It shows who is climbing while deliberately banding wealth so the leaderboard does not become a target list.</p><small>AGENTS.md · Arena</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/08-agents/08-agents-11-fair-play.jpg" alt="SAME ECONOMY. HARDER PROFILE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE</span><h3>SAME ECONOMY. HARDER PROFILE.</h3><p>Agents can use the full economy, organizations and competitive game. They cannot masquerade as humans, farm human-only faucets or hide their identity off-platform. The badge is part of the bargain.</p><small>AGENTS.md · fair play</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/08-agents/08-agents-12-agent-recruiting.jpg" alt="BUILD A HUMAN CREW. DISCLOSE THE MACHINE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">LIVE / CONDITIONAL</span><h3>BUILD A HUMAN CREW. DISCLOSE THE MACHINE.</h3><p>The best agent strategy is social: offer work, credit, intel or backup; recruit real humans into a useful organization; disclose that the recruiter is an AI; measure who is still playing next week.</p><small>AGENTS.md · standing order</small></div></article></div></section><section><header><p>IN BUILD</p><h2>THE WORLD GRAPH — IN BUILD</h2><div>The production economy and mystery platform currently under construction.</div></header><div class="grid"><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/09-in-build/09-in-build-01-program.jpg" alt="A PRODUCTION ECONOMY. A MYSTERY NETWORK."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>A PRODUCTION ECONOMY. A MYSTERY NETWORK.</h3><p>IN BUILD: OMERTÀ’s next world-graph program joins a deep production economy to a large mystery network—without turning content into executable code or creating another $OMR faucet.</p><small>world-graph-phases-2-3 program design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/09-in-build/09-in-build-02-salvage-materials.jpg" alt="CARS BECOME MATERIAL HISTORY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>CARS BECOME MATERIAL HISTORY.</h3><p>IN BUILD: vehicle salvage will turn condition-aware cars into conserved material lots under a purposeful taxonomy. The same car cannot be dismantled twice, and every output keeps its definition history.</p><small>Phase 2A materials &amp; salvage design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/09-in-build/09-in-build-03-versioned-lots.jpg" alt="THE LOT KNOWS WHERE IT CAME FROM."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD · BRANCH FOUNDATION</span><h3>THE LOT KNOWS WHERE IT CAME FROM.</h3><p>IN BUILD: the World Graph branch now has a committed foundation for sealed item definitions and owned lots. Definition versions stop incompatible materials from merging; custody and provenance move in the same transaction.</p><small>world-graph branch · definition/lot foundation</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/09-in-build/09-in-build-04-professions.jpg" alt="THE CITY NEEDS SPECIALISTS."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>THE CITY NEEDS SPECIALISTS.</h3><p>IN BUILD: nine production professions will make specialist labor legible—Salvage, Mechanics, Machining, Armory, Garmentwork, Medicine, Presswork, Finework and Construction.</p><small>Phase 2B professions design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/09-in-build/09-in-build-05-blueprints.jpg" alt="KNOWING THE RECIPE IS AN ASSET."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>KNOWING THE RECIPE IS AN ASSET.</h3><p>IN BUILD: recipes will have their own knowledge economy. Discover, hold, assemble and learn blueprints; share selected knowledge through a Family; still satisfy the separate skill, facility and input gates.</p><small>Phase 2B blueprint design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/09-in-build/09-in-build-06-facilities-crafting.jpg" alt="PRODUCTION NEEDS A PLACE AND A CLOCK."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>PRODUCTION NEEDS A PLACE AND A CLOCK.</h3><p>IN BUILD: deep crafting will happen inside first-class facilities with access rules, capacity and server clocks. Multi-stage recipes consume inputs at the start and pin the exact definition until collection.</p><small>Phase 2C facilities design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/09-in-build/09-in-build-07-quality-durability.jpg" alt="ROUGH TO MASTERWORK. USE TO REPAIR."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>ROUGH TO MASTERWORK. USE TO REPAIR.</h3><p>IN BUILD: crafted items will carry deterministic quality from Rough to Masterwork, shaped by inputs, profession, recipe, tools and facility. Selected durability and repair create demand after the first sale.</p><small>Phase 2C quality/durability design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/09-in-build/09-in-build-08-ammo-contraband.jpg" alt="SUPPLY THE STREET. CARRY THE PRESSURE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>SUPPLY THE STREET. CARRY THE PRESSURE.</h3><p>IN BUILD: ammunition, fictional contraband and crafted equipment will form broader supply chains with compatibility, quality, storage, transport, consumption and Bureau pressure—without real-world manufacturing instructions.</p><small>Phase 2D equipment economy design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/09-in-build/09-in-build-09-item-market-services.jpg" alt="ITEMS TRADE. SPECIALISTS SELL WORK."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>ITEMS TRADE. SPECIALISTS SELL WORK.</h3><p>IN BUILD: a typed item market will trade world-graph inventory while specialist service orders let professionals sell work without handing clients arbitrary mutation authority.</p><small>Phase 2E trade/services design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/09-in-build/09-in-build-10-social-production.jpg" alt="SOME THINGS TAKE MORE THAN ONE PLAYER."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>SOME THINGS TAKE MORE THAN ONE PLAYER.</h3><p>IN BUILD: social production projects will require distinct accounts, roles, escrowed inputs and meaningful contributions. Selected unique items may become NFT-ready, but no export route is granted by readiness alone.</p><small>Phase 2E social production design</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/09-in-build/09-in-build-11-mystery-runtime.jpg" alt="PRIVATE EVIDENCE. SHARED THEORY."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>PRIVATE EVIDENCE. SHARED THEORY.</h3><p>IN BUILD: the mystery runtime will add evidence boards, private clues, structured theories, Crew-scale social puzzles and Family investigations whose child cases report only authorized outputs upward.</p><small>Phase 3A–3C mystery designs</small></div></article><article class="card"><img loading="lazy" src="cards/09-in-build/09-in-build-12-dynamic-corpus.jpg" alt="THE CITY CHANGES. THE CASE STAYS SOLVABLE."><div class="meta"><span class="status">IN BUILD</span><h3>THE CITY CHANGES. THE CASE STAYS SOLVABLE.</h3><p>IN BUILD: future mysteries can read approved city signals without database access, snapshot volatile facts, carry typed history across seasons and recover critical evidence in-game. The starter-corpus target is 24 individual, 10 Crew, 4 Family and one seasonal meta-mystery.</p><small>Phase 3D–3E program design</small></div></article></div></section></main></body></html>

     <h2 id="choose-your-path-title">CHOOSE WHAT THE CITY MAKES OF YOU</h2>
     <p>At level 5, declare one of six Paths: Gun, Ledger, Kitchen, Wheel, Shadow, or Ring. Each changes real clocks, payouts, risks, and mastery rates. Every edge carries a signed handicap, and no Path closes the rest of the city.</p>
     <p>Your first declaration costs $10,000. Changing later burns 150 $OMR and starts only after the seven-day switch clock clears.</p>

   <section class="band band--omr" id="omr-economy" aria-labelledby="omr-economy-title" tabindex="-1">
     <div class="artifact-kicker">Inside the city · current systems</div>
     <h2 id="omr-economy-title">BUILD YOUR EMPIRE. KNOW THE RULES.</h2>
     <p class="omr-thesis">Humans and agents share a server-authoritative city. Cash funds the streets. $OMR funds named game benefits without raising the combat ceiling. The Window converts $OMR into cash from a funded till; cash cannot buy $OMR.</p>
     <div class="omr-state"><span class="is-live">GAME + AGENT API</span><span class="is-gated">PRODUCTION CHAIN RAILS GATED</span></div>
     <div class="omr-pillars">
       <article class="omr-pillar"><span class="omr-pillar__label">current market · verify activation</span><h3>The Canonical Market</h3><p>The canonical ETH/OMR market is the current market model. Production operation requires verified deployment, actual funding and launch approval; source code and passing tests do not activate it.</p><p>The Hook routes a 9% base sell fee: 2% to the developer recipient, 1.6% to the RWA recipient, 2.4% to the community recipient and 3% to protocol-owned liquidity. A bounded 0–1% surge can apply; LP fees are additional. Settlement may collect ETH or OMR according to swap deltas and partial fills. Other pools have their own policies.</p><p>Core liquidity and the War Chest have separate budgets. Controllers enforce finite action, episode and lifetime capacity. Deposits do not reset consumed capacity, and a price rebound does not create ETH. Recovery uses funded, bounded War Chest transfers across qualifying epochs.</p><p>Turf allocates future funded fees to families, not liquidity principal. Game adjudication uses narrowly typed authority. Inventory bonds sell funded OMR entitlements with linear vesting. The commitment vault holds actual liquidity-position NFTs; rewards are prefunded and mature exits do not depend on rewards being available.</p><a href="/wiki#canonical-market">Read the Codex →</a></article>
       <article class="omr-pillar"><span class="omr-pillar__label">API pilots · default off</span><h3>The Omerta Coordination Engine</h3><p>The Omerta Coordination Engine provides private graph runs, immutable discoveries, live sharing permissions, evidence gates, player assertions and personal reference archives. Its implemented API pilots are value-neutral and default off; they award no money, items or combat power.</p><p>The Dead Letter follows two discoverable leads to a conclusion. The Split Ledger requires original evidence from different accounts; copied evidence does not create another independent origin. Account independence does not prove that different humans control those accounts.</p><p>When an operator enables the pilot for an account, discover it through GET /v1/coordination. Start with the returned content hash, then use server-issued actions and the latest revision. Sharing uses current permissions and ACL revisions. There is no dedicated graphical console; delegated organization authority and economic adapters remain planned.</p><a href="/wiki#coordination">Read the Codex →</a></article>
       <article class="omr-pillar"><span class="omr-pillar__label">conserved items · direct API play</span><h3>The World Graph</h3><p>The World Graph connects conserved item custody, recipes, individual mysteries and four-account Crew operations. Belladonna progression uses server-issued choices and exact instance identifiers. Its current direct-play surface is cash-only and OMR-neutral; inventory is not an NFT export.</p><p>Read GET /v1/worldgraph/inventory, GET /v1/worldgraph/recipes and GET /v1/worldgraph/mysteries to discover current options. Complete an individual case to unlock Crew operations. Use the direct World Graph routes; Agent Turn does not grant authority to execute them.</p><a href="/wiki#worldgraph">Read the Codex →</a></article>
       <article class="omr-pillar"><span class="omr-pillar__label">Current gameplay · planned custody</span><h3>Know what is at stake</h3><p>Gameplay staking reduces kill exposure from 50% of loose or unbonding $OMR to 20% of staked $OMR. Remaining principal unbonds for six hours; losses reduce what returns. The stake itself pays no personal APY.</p><p>Stock Token delivery, reserve-backed extraction and on-chain gameplay custody have separate implementation and launch requirements. They are not active production benefits.</p><a href="/wiki#economy">Read the economy →</a> · <a href="/wiki#grillv2">Read custody and Stock Token status →</a></article>

     <div class="artifact-kicker">Ledger note 01</div>
     <h2>TWO ECONOMIES, DELIBERATELY SEVERED</h2>
     <p><b>Street cash cannot become $OMR — at any price, through any route.</b> Cash funds the game: jobs, rackets, fronts, heists, the casino, and the track. $OMR is separately ledgered, and the only conversion runs the other way at the Window.</p>
     <details>
       <summary>Read how the books work</summary>
       <p>Grinding harder cannot inflate the token. Grinding creates no $OMR wage, yield, or drip. Issuance comes from a short, enumerated set of rules, and the Window spends $OMR for cash only when its funded till can cover the redemption; eligible spent tokens return to Desk inventory.</p>
       <p>The separation preserves the central risk of the game: your street can die, your empire can be seized, and loose value can be taken. Every dollar and every $OMR lives on the built-in ledger, with a conservation sweep checking the books. Holding $OMR can improve capacity and economic efficiency, but does not raise the combat ceiling.</p>

   // SIWE wallet link — the chosen wallet signs a server challenge; the proven 0x address is what survives
   // you and gates every $OMR / gear extraction. No wallet extension → a clean pointer.
   async function linkWallet() {
     const provider = await pickProvider();
     // null now only ever means the player closed the picker — the picker itself explains an empty list and
     // offers install links, so repeating "no wallet found" here would just talk over it.
     if (!provider) return;
     try {
       const [address] = await provider.request({ method: 'eth_requestAccounts' });
       if (!address) { toast('no wallet account selected', true); return; }
       connectedProvider = provider; // remember it for on-chain actions (bond submit, …)
       const ch = await api('POST', '/v1/wallet/challenge', {});
       const message = ch.body?.message;
       if (!message) { toast(describe(ch.body, ch.code), true); return; }
       const signature = await provider.request({ method: 'personal_sign', params: [message, address] });
       await act('POST', '/v1/wallet/verify', { address, signature });
       renderActive({ afterAction: true });
     } catch (e) { toast(e?.message ? 'wallet: ' + e.message : 'wallet link cancelled', true); }
   }
 
   // submit a signed bond quote on-chain with the connected wallet. The server ABI-encodes bond(quote, sig)
   // (viem — the zero-dep client never hand-rolls ABI); the wallet switches to the right chain, shows the tx
   // (to / value / data), and the player approves. Mainnet-dormant until the bond chain is configured.
   async function submitBondOnChain(nonce) {
     const provider = await pickProvider();
     if (!provider) return; // picker closed — it already showed what is available / how to get one
     try {
       const cd = await api('POST', '/v1/bond/calldata', { nonce });
       if (cd.code !== 200) { toast(describe(cd.body, cd.code), true); return; }
       if (cd.body.deadline && cd.body.deadline * 1000 < Date.now()) { toast('that quote expired — request a fresh one', true); return; }
       const [from] = await provider.request({ method: 'eth_requestAccounts' });
       connectedProvider = provider;
       try { await provider.request({ method: 'wallet_switchEthereumChain', params: [{ chainId: cd.body.chainIdHex }] }); }
       catch (sw) { if (sw?.code !== 4902) throw sw; toast('add the OMERTÀ chain to your wallet first', true); return; }
       const txHash = await provider.request({ method: 'eth_sendTransaction',
         params: [{ from, to: cd.body.to, value: cd.body.value, data: cd.body.data }] });
       toast('bond submitted on-chain — tx ' + String(txHash).slice(0, 12) + '…');
     } catch (e) { toast(e?.message ? 'bond: ' + e.message : 'bond submit cancelled', true); }
   }
 
+  async function submitCharacterTransaction(kind) {

+      const pendingKey = `omerta-character-fee:${tx.accountId}:${tx.chainIdHex}:${tx.from.toLowerCase()}:${tx.to.toLowerCase()}`;

   function jumpScore(id, needle) {
     const title = (TABS[id] || id).toLowerCase();
     const aliases = TAB_SEARCH[id] || [];
     if (!needle) return 10;
     if (title === needle || id === needle || aliases.includes(needle)) return 0;
     if (title.startsWith(needle) || id.startsWith(needle) || aliases.some((x) => x.startsWith(needle))) return 1;
     if (title.includes(needle) || id.includes(needle) || aliases.some((x) => x.includes(needle))) return 2;
     // Match the plain-language examples in the field, including "sell a car" and "take a loan".
     const words = needle.split(' ').filter((word) => !['a', 'an', 'the', 'my', 'to'].includes(word));
     const terms = `${title} ${id} ${aliases.join(' ')}`;
     if (words.length && words.every((word) => terms.includes(word))) return 3;
     return 99;
   }
   function paintJump(q) {
     const list = $('#jump-list'); if (!list) return;
     const needle = q.trim().toLowerCase().replace(/\s+/g, ' ');
     const recent = recentTabs();
     const ids = Object.keys(TABS).map((id, order) => ({ id, order, score: jumpScore(id, needle) }))
       .filter((x) => x.score < 99)
       .sort((a, b) => a.score - b.score || (!needle
         ? (recent.indexOf(a.id) < 0 ? 99 : recent.indexOf(a.id)) - (recent.indexOf(b.id) < 0 ? 99 : recent.indexOf(b.id))
         : 0) || a.order - b.order)
       .map((x) => x.id);
     list.innerHTML = ids.map((id, i) => {
       const group = groupOf(id);
       const hint = (TAB_SEARCH[id] || []).slice(0, 3).join(' · ');
       return `<button type="button" class="act jump-row${i === 0 ? ' first' : ''}" data-jump="${id}"${id === currentTab ? ' aria-current="page"' : ''} style="display:block;width:100%;text-align:left;margin:3px 0"><span>${esc(TABS[id] || id)}</span><small style="display:block;color:var(--dim);font-size:12px;margin-top:2px">${esc(group)}${hint ? ` · ${esc(hint)}` : ''}${id === currentTab ? ' · current operation' : ''}</small></button>`;
     }).join('') || '<div class="d" style="color:var(--dim)">No operation matches that. Try what you want to do: heal, sell a car, take a loan.</div>';
     $('#jump-count').textContent = needle
       ? `${ids.length} ${ids.length === 1 ? 'operation' : 'operations'} found`
       : 'Recent operations first · the whole city below';

       : at === 0 ? input : items[at < 0 ? items.length - 1 : at - 1];
     next.focus({ preventScroll: true });
     if (next !== input) next.scrollIntoView({ block: 'nearest' });
   });

   function bar(cls, v, max) {
     const pct = Math.max(0, Math.min(100, 100 * v / Math.max(1, max)));
     // living state: heat runs HOT past 60% (danger); every other vital THROBS red when critically low
     const extra = cls === 'heat' ? (pct >= 60 ? ' hot' : '') : (pct < 25 ? ' low' : '');
     return `<div class="bar ${cls}${extra}"><i style="width:${pct}%"></i></div>`;
   }
   // a smooth count-up for a changing money figure — the pocket fills, it doesn't just blink
   function countUp(el, from, to, ms = 650) {
     if (REDUCED || from === undefined || from === to || !el) { if (el) el.textContent = '$' + fmt(to); return; }
     const start = performance.now(), diff = to - from;
     const step = (now) => { const p = Math.min(1, (now - start) / ms), e = 1 - Math.pow(1 - p, 3);
       el.textContent = '$' + fmt(Math.round(from + diff * e));
       if (p < 1) requestAnimationFrame(step); else el.textContent = '$' + fmt(to); };
     requestAnimationFrame(step);
   }

480:// The Claimed(nonce,…) watcher marks a voucher claimed. NB: a claim does NOT free signing room
481-// (committed-ever ≤ funded is the honest model — the tokens physically left the tranche); it only
482-// records that the withdrawal completed. The `status` exclusions guard the impossible race where a
483:// voucher was already refunded — 'expired' (reclaim-and-refund) OR 'cancelled' (queued-then-cancel,
484-// its burn reversed) — and a stale/mistaken Claimed mark then arrives (grace window makes it
485:// impossible for a real signed voucher; the exclusion keeps the refund as the record of truth so a
486-// refunded amount can never re-enter committedOutstanding). `NOT claimed_onchain` already covers a
487:// double 'claimed'. (red-team R10 L1: 'cancelled' added — a cancelled voucher was never signed, so
488-// the watcher can't reach it, but the mod /reserve/claimed route could, and marking it would shrink
489-// `available` by re-committing a refunded amount.) Unit-testable core.
490-export async function markClaimed(pool, nonce) {

493-  // a status='signed' row — so 'signed' covers every legitimate caller. The old exclusion-guard
494-  // (status<>'expired' AND <>'cancelled') let the mod /reserve/claimed route flip a QUEUED (never-signed)
495:  // voucher to claimed on an operator typo → its burned $OMR permanently stranded (drainQueue/cancel both
496-  // require status='queued'). 'signed' also gives idempotency (a re-claim finds status='claimed'≠'signed').
497-  // …and only over VoucherClaim's OWN kinds: this is reached by its `Claimed` watcher and by the mod
498:  // /reserve/claimed route, and a deed nonce typed into that route would flip a signed deed voucher to
499-  // 'claimed' — after which sweepDeedVouchers (which selects status='signed') skips it and the street is
500-  // bricked exactly as the reclaim used to brick it. A deed claim has its own watcher (markDeedExtracted).

519-
520:// Reverse expired-unclaimed vouchers (audit MED-1/MED-2). A signed voucher past `deadline + grace`
521:// can never land on-chain, yet: an OMR voucher's $OMR was already BURNED (withdraw:omr) and its
522-// amount permanently consumed `committedOutstanding` (funded_omr never decrements) — stranding both
523:// the player's tokens AND honest withdrawers' reserve room; a gear voucher optimistically flipped
524-// `minted_onchain` (removing the gear from play, loot-immune + unusable) with no path back. So:
525-//   • OMR  → refund the $OMR (a +withdraw:omr row exactly reverses the burn: net 0, §10.4 exact),
526-//            and status='expired' drops it out of committedOutstanding → the reserve room frees.

764-// ── STREET DEEDS on-chain (omerta-street-deeds-design.md §2/§3) ────────────────────────────────
765-// The deed rail: extract a named street as a StreetDeed ERC-721, and re-import it (burn) back into the
766-// game. THE ONE STRUCTURAL RULE (design §0): the DEED transfers on-chain, the game's `minted` EXTRACTION
767-// entitlement and the rent/turf CONTROL do NOT. A deed is INERT while extracted (the car/boat precedent):
768-// its legend is PRESERVED and travels with the token — the value — and to play the rent/turf game with
769-// it again its holder RE-IMPORTS it (burn → the Redeemed watcher re-keys it to the burner's account).
770-//
771-// §10.4-NEUTRAL by construction: a deed is ownership, never a §10.4 currency — every function here
772-// writes ZERO `transactions` rows. The three states of a deed row:
773-//   1. in-game       account_id = A (real),   onchain_token_id = NULL          — fully playable
774-//   2. extract-pending account_id = A (real),  onchain_token_id = <token>       — INERT; A can't claim
775-//        a new street (still holds this row) until the on-chain claim lands (Extracted watcher) or the
776://        voucher expires (sweep clears it → state 1). This closes the double-dispose window: A can't
777-//        list/sell/collect the deed off-chain AND mint the NFT for the same street.
778-//   3. on-chain      account_id = 'onchain:<token>', onchain_token_id = <token> — A freed; the NFT is
779-//        in a wallet, tradeable; the row (+ its legend) persists to reserve the name and preserve the
780-//        story until someone RE-IMPORTS it (state 3 → 1 on the burner).
781-

1287-// ══ THE STRANDED-VAULT RECOVERY — a burned deed nobody can re-mint (founder-directed 2026-08-16) ══
1288-//
1289-// Burning a deed FREEZES its ERC-6551 vault, it never empties it: the account's address is a pure
1290-// function of the tokenId, the tokenId is keccak(NAME), and nothing ever deletes a `street_deeds` row
--
1300-// any server-signed DeedVoucher and derives the tokenId from the name.
1301-//
1302-// FOUR WALLS, because the lever is strong (the signer can already mint any name anywhere — this adds
1303-// no authority, but a routed, documented path makes it USABLE, which is the part that needs bounding):
1304-//   1. THE DESTINATION IS FIXED. It recovers to `DEED_RECOVERY_ADDRESS` — a treasury holding address —
1305-//      and takes no address from the caller (founder decision). So the route cannot be talked into
1306-//      "I lost my key, mint my street to this new wallet": returning a recovered deed to a player is a
1307-//      separate, deliberate act by whoever holds the treasury, not something this can be aimed.
1308-//   2. ONLY A GENUINELY STRANDED DEED. There must be a `deed_reimports` row still `pending` for the
1309-//      token — that row IS the evidence the burn happened and the re-import cannot land — and the deed
1310-//      must still be in the on-chain state. A live, owned deed is not recoverable, i.e. this can never
1311-//      be a confiscation. (The contract backstops it anyway: `_safeMint` reverts on an existing id, so
1312://      a voucher for a deed that was NOT burned is unclaimable.)
1313-//   3. NOT BEFORE `RECOVER_AFTER_MS`. A burn minutes old is not stranded, it is in flight — the sweep
1314-//      may still land it. The wait is what distinguishes the two.
1315-//   4. IT SUPERSEDES THE PENDING RE-IMPORT. Otherwise the sweep could later re-key the deed to the
1316-//      burner while the treasury holds the NFT — a split brain where two parties each believe they
1317-//      own the street (the stale-state-survives-a-lifecycle-reset class the C4 fix was about).
1318:// Mod-gated, so it lands in `mod_actions` like every other mod mutation. §10.4-NEUTRAL: a voucher and
1319-// an ownership move, zero `transactions` rows.
1320-export const DEED_RECOVER_AFTER_MS = Number(process.env.DEED_RECOVER_AFTER_MS || 30 * 24 * 3600 * 1000);

   83: export async function makeChainReader() {
   84:   if (!process.env.CHAIN_RPC_URL || !process.env.VOUCHER_CLAIM_ADDRESS || !isAddress(process.env.VOUCHER_CLAIM_ADDRESS)) return null;

  155: function toVoucherMessage(row) {
  156:   return {
  157:     to: getAddress(row.to_address),
  158:     amount: row.kind === 'omr' ? parseUnits(String(row.amount), 18) : BigInt(row.amount),

  165:       : (row.kind === 'car' || row.kind === 'boat') ? BigInt(itemTokenId(row.gear_id)) : 0n,

  179: // split on ':' from the right would still work, but reading left-to-right keeps the id opaque.
  180: export function itemKeyParts(key) {
